| LOA-06 | A student may file an LOA if the student withdraws after two weeks of classes have elapsed; the student is subject to a withdrawal fee from the Cashier's office. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-07 | The Dean, Associate Dean, or the duly authorized representative informs the University Registrar of the leave, indicating the reasons and the amount of money refunded to the student, if any. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-08 | For a leave availed of by the student during the second half of the semester, the class standing of the courses enrolled is W (withdrawn); no refund of tuition and fees is provided. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-09 | No leave of absence is granted during the semester within two (2) weeks before the last day of classes. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-10 | A master's program must be finished within seven academic years and a doctoral program within nine academic years, including the leave of absence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| LOA-11 | A student who withdraws from the college without a formal leave of absence is considered absent without leave (AWOL) and has registration privileges curtailed or entirely withdrawn. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-12 | The student may file the leave through a structured form in place of a letter, provided the Dean accepts the form as the written request. | Group working notes, Consultation 20 July 2026 rehearsal (structured form suggested by the adviser) | Proposed |
| LOA-13 | The platform checks a leave request for an allowed reason category, a written reason, a valid start and end semester (at most two semesters per request and four in total with earlier approved leave), the filing date (not within fourteen days before the last day of classes) and the prototype minimum of one completed semester, and gives the Dean a recommendation; the Dean decides. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): LOA policy review | Proposed |

#### 4.6.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- A leave in the second half of the semester: class standing is W and no refund is given (LOA-08). A leave requested within two weeks before the last day of classes is not granted (LOA-09).
- The leave ends and the student does not return: the student is treated as absent without leave (see 4.8) unless a further renewal within the two-year limit has been approved (LOA-03).
- The student wants to withdraw a pending leave request before the decision: the sources do not say whether this is allowed. The platform allows it while the request is Submitted or in Dean Review, as the adviser suggested (Consultation 20 July 2026); the student may then file a new request (OQ-12).
- A student who stopped attending without filing a leave: see 4.8, absent without leave.

#### 4.6.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-07 Application for Leave of Absence. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Written leave request and Dean's decision | Dean's office; GS staff record |
| Notice of leave with reasons and refund to the Registrar | University Registrar |
| Student status On Leave with effective dates | Graduate School record |

#### 4.6.8 How the platform supports this process

The student files a structured leave request in the portal: a reason category, the reason, and the start and end semester. There is no letter or document to upload. The platform blocks the submission when the request covers more than the register allows for one request (2 semesters, loa.max_period_semesters), when the semesters already approved plus the request would pass 4 semesters in total (loa.max_total_semesters, renewals included), when the start semester is already over or is running with 14 days or fewer before its last day of classes (loa.no_filing_days_before_term_end), or when another leave request is already in progress. A request filed in the second half of a running semester is accepted with a note that the enrolled courses become W and no refund is given; the platform only shows that note, it does not mark the courses W or work out a refund. The number of earlier leaves is not limited, only the semesters. The platform also estimates whether the student has completed the prototype minimum of one semester; a shortfall is shown to the Dean as Needs Review and blocks nothing. Until the Dean decides, the student can withdraw a pending request (Submitted or Dean Review) and file a new one. GS staff review the same checks and forward the request to the Dean, who approves, denies or returns it for revision. At approval the two-week check (no leave is granted within the last two weeks of the semester) runs again, and the Dean cannot approve a request that fails it. Approval sets the status On Leave, which also stops the student being enrolled in subjects, and staff can download a provisional notice for the Registrar (period and reason category; no refund amount). The residency clock keeps running during the leave.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform now follows the Handbook on the points where the earlier build had differed: a leave of up to one year (two semesters) per request, renewable up to four semesters in total, no limit on the number of leaves, a leave allowed in the second half of a semester (with W and no refund shown) but not within the last two weeks, and time on leave counted inside the maximum residence (the residency clock is never paused). Differences remain. The Handbook's filing deadline is not stated (LOA-04), so the platform applies only the two date checks above. The withdrawal fee after two weeks (LOA-06), the refund amount in the notice to the Registrar (LOA-07) and the marking of courses W (LOA-08) are not computed. A semester is counted by its position in the list of semesters set up in the platform, so a year of leave is two semesters, not three terms (OQ-09). The Dean's approval sets On Leave at once, not from the start semester. It runs the two-week no-grant check again, but not the other limit checks. The student form lists only future semesters, although the platform's check would also accept a semester that is running (OQ-11). These points are open questions OQ-09 to OQ-12; the Handbook is in force.

### 4.7 Return from leave of absence and readmission

#### 4.7.1 Purpose

To state how a student who was on leave of absence, or who was absent without leave, returns to the program.

#### 4.7.2 Who is involved

- Student
- GS staff
- Graduate School Dean
- University Registrar (external)
- Business Office (external)

#### 4.7.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** The approved leave period ends, or an absent student wants to resume.

**Preconditions.**

- The student is within the maximum residence limit (see 4.9), or accepts the extension conditions.
- The student has not failed a subject (failure means no re-admission to the program; DRP-06).

#### 4.7.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Student | Declares the intention to enroll by writing to the University Registrar through the Graduate School Dean. | Written return intention | Before the semester of return (date not stated) |
| 2† | GS staff | Records the request, checks the target return term and the previous leave period, and forwards it to the Dean. | Readmission request record | — |
| 3 | Student | Obtains a clearance from the Business Office and attends the interview (reason for the delay, residency requirements). | Business Office clearance; interview | — |
| 4 | Academic Coordinator / Dean | Evaluates the subject and grade requirements; the Dean issues the Advise Slip. | Advise Slip from the Dean | — |
| 5 | Dean | Approves or denies the readmission and endorses the student's written intent to the University Registrar. | Dean's decision; endorsement to the Registrar | — |
| 6† | GS staff | Records the student as Active for the return term and notifies the student. | Student status: Active | After the Dean's decision |

† This step rests on a rule marked Practice or Proposed in section 4.7.5, or on the group's process model; it is not described in the Handbook or the Research Protocol and is to be confirmed by the Graduate School.

#### 4.7.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| RDM-01 | A student returning from a leave of absence (LOA) or absence without leave (AWOL) declares his or her intention to enroll by writing to the University Registrar through the Graduate School Dean, who then endorses it to the University Registrar. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| RDM-02 | A returnee must present: (1) clearance from the Business Office, (2) an interview (reason for delay, residency requirements), (3) evaluation of subject and grade requirements, (4) an Advise Slip from the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 45 | Official |
| RDM-03 | Failure in any subject means no re-admission to the program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RDM-04 | A student who cannot finish within the maximum residence (seven years for a master's, nine for a doctorate) must re-enroll all courses taken and earn new credits. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RDM-05 | The platform's readmission checklist asks for a structured return intention, a confirmed updated study plan, a program or adviser consultation, and confirmation that no accountability is pending. The Handbook's four requirements (RDM-02) are the official list. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): readmission requirements | Proposed |

#### 4.7.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- The return term is outside the maximum residence: the student is classified as within the limit, in the extension (two more years with a graded six-unit refresher course), or needing full re-enrollment (see 4.9).
- The student was absent without leave: the same written declaration is used (RDM-01).
- The student was dropped for failing a subject: no re-admission (RDM-03).

#### 4.7.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-08 Application for Readmission (Return from Leave or AWOL). Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Written return intention and the Dean's endorsement | Dean's office; University Registrar |
| Business Office clearance; interview notes; Advise Slip | Graduate School record |
| Student status: Active from the return term | Graduate School record |

#### 4.7.8 How the platform supports this process

The student files a structured return request in the portal: a return semester that starts after today, the start and end of the previous leave, a written return intention, and four ticked confirmations (a structured return intention, an updated study plan, a consultation with the program or the adviser, and no pending accountability). The ticks are the student's own statement; nothing is uploaded or verified. GS staff review the request, confirm the Business Office clearance and the interview, and forward it to the Dean; staff cannot forward it until both are ticked. The review marks Needs Review when the student is not on leave, but does not block the request. The Dean cannot approve before staff have confirmed both the Business Office clearance and the interview (an older record that lacks them is refused with a message naming what is missing). The Dean approves, denies or returns it for revision. Approval sets the status Active, tags the student Not Enrolled for the return term and opens a task for the Academic Coordinator on the study plan and enrollment; a denial leaves the record as it was. Staff can download a provisional file for the Registrar with the return semester and the previous leave period.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform's four-item checklist differs from the Handbook's four requirements (RDM-02). The platform records the staff confirmation of the Business Office clearance and the interview, and the Dean cannot approve without them. When the Dean approves, the platform produces the Advise Slip as a print page for staff, the Dean and the student (I-304). It does not check failed subjects (RDM-03) or the maximum residence for a return from leave (RDM-04); these are left to people. The classification by years in the program is applied only to a return from AWOL (see 4.8), not to a return from leave. The portal does not require that the student is on leave before the request is filed; staff see a flag when the student is not.

### 4.8 Absence without leave (AWOL)

#### 4.8.1 Purpose

To state what happens to a student who stops enrolling without a formal leave of absence.

#### 4.8.2 Who is involved

- Student
- GS staff
- Graduate School Dean
- University Registrar (external)

#### 4.8.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** A student withdraws from the University, or stops enrolling, without a formal leave of absence.

**Preconditions.**

- No approved leave of absence covers the period.

#### 4.8.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1† | GS staff | Notices that the student has no approved leave and no enrollment for a semester, and marks the student as absent without leave after checking the official record. | AWOL status (source evidence) | — |
| 2 | Registrar | Curtails or withdraws the student's registration privileges. | Registration privileges | — |
| 3 | Student | Returns by declaring the intention to enroll in writing through the Graduate School Dean (see 4.7). | Written return intention | — |
| 4 | Dean | Endorses the written intent to the University Registrar; the maximum residence classification of the student decides the conditions of return. | Endorsement; classification | — |

† This step rests on a rule marked Practice or Proposed in section 4.8.5, or on the group's process model; it is not described in the Handbook or the Research Protocol and is to be confirmed by the Graduate School.

#### 4.8.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AWL-01 | A student who withdraws from a college without a formal leave of absence is considered in "absent without leave" status (AWOL) and has registration privileges curtailed or entirely withdrawn. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| AWL-02 | A student returning from AWOL declares the intention to enroll by writing to the University Registrar through the Graduate School Dean, who endorses it to the Registrar. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| AWL-03 | A returnee presents a Business Office clearance, an interview, an evaluation of subject and grade requirements and an Advise Slip from the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 45 | Official |
| AWL-04 | An AWOL status has the same effect as a leave of absence for reporting: it is communicated to the Registrar. | Consultation 20 July 2026, adviser's statement; not confirmed by Graduate School staff | Proposed |
| AWL-05 | AWOL is flagged from source evidence (an imported AWOL standing, or a semester enrollment marked Withdrawn with no approved leave and no resolved return), not typed in by a user and not inferred from absence alone. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): AWOL policy review | Proposed |
| AWL-06 | On return, the student is classified by years in the program: within the normal limit (five years master's, seven years doctorate), extension with a graded six-unit refresher (up to seven and nine), or full re-enrollment beyond that. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 54-55 | Proposed |

#### 4.8.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- It is not clear whether the period of AWOL counts toward maximum residence; the Handbook counts LOA (LOA-10) but is silent about AWOL (OQ-14).
- A student with an approved leave is never AWOL; a student whose leave lapsed without return may become AWOL (Proposed; the platform does not do this automatically).

#### 4.8.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-09 Notice of Absence Without Leave (AWOL). Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| AWOL status and its source evidence | Graduate School record |
| Written return intention and endorsement | Dean's office; University Registrar |

#### 4.8.8 How the platform supports this process

The platform flags a student as AWOL from evidence only and refuses an attempt by a user to declare AWOL. The evidence is an imported AWOL standing or tag, or a latest semester enrollment marked Withdrawn when the student has no leave of absence and no resolved return case; absence alone is not taken as evidence. When a student is flagged the platform sets the status, raises a monitoring alert, opens a task for the Academic Coordinator and stops the student being enrolled in subjects. To return, the student files a structured declaration (a future return semester, the last semester enrolled, a written intent and a reason); staff forward it, and cannot do so until both the Business Office clearance and the interview are ticked, and the Dean decides. As for a readmission, the Dean cannot approve a return from AWOL before staff have confirmed the Business Office clearance and the interview. The outcome depends on the student's years in the program measured against the register: within the normal limit (5 years for a master's, 7 for a doctorate) the return is approved and the student becomes Active; within the extension (up to 7 and 9 years) the return is approved with a graded six-unit refresher required and a task is opened; beyond that the student stays AWOL and a task for a re-enrollment evaluation is opened. After the Dean approves, staff can download a provisional file for the Registrar.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The Handbook does not say how the Graduate School learns of an AWOL or who declares it (OQ-14); the platform's rule is the evidence above. A leave that lapsed without a return is not turned into AWOL automatically. The AWOL period counts toward the maximum residence, because the residency clock is never paused. The Business Office clearance and the interview (AWL-03) are confirmed by staff before the Dean can approve; the Advise Slip is not produced (planned, I-304). Years are counted in academic years from the student's entry academic year, which is approximate (see 4.9).

### 4.9 Residency enrollment and maximum residence

#### 4.9.1 Purpose

To state when a student with no subjects must still enroll (residency) and the longest time a student may take to finish a program.

#### 4.9.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Academic Coordinator
- GS staff
- Graduate School Dean

#### 4.9.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** A student has finished all coursework and is working on the thesis, dissertation or practicum; or is not enrolled in any subject but is completing an INC, taking the comprehensive examination, or waiting for a research publication.

**Preconditions.**

- The student has finished all coursework (except for completing an INC or taking the comprehensive examination).

#### 4.9.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Academic Coordinator | For the comprehensive examination, makes the initial evaluation of the student's courses and advises the student to enroll in Residency in Comprehensive Examination. | Initial evaluation | — |
| 2 | Student | Registers for residence within the semester, for the purpose that applies (thesis or dissertation writing, practicum or internship, completing an INC, comprehensive examination, waiting for publication). | Residency enrollment | Within the semester |
| 3† | GS staff | Records the residency enrollment with its purpose and checks that the student is not simply skipping a semester (a student who does not intend to enroll should file a leave of absence). | Residency record | — |
| 4† | GS staff | Watches the student's years in the program against the maximum residence. | Residency watchlist | — |

† This step rests on a rule marked Practice or Proposed in section 4.9.5, or on the group's process model; it is not described in the Handbook or the Research Protocol and is to be confirmed by the Graduate School.

#### 4.9.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| RES-01 | Students who have finished all their coursework and are just working on their thesis or dissertation and practicum or internship, after a semester of enrollment for thesis writing and practicum, may enroll for residence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-02 | Students who are not enrolled in any subject but who want to complete an INC, will take the comprehensive exam, or are waiting for their research publication must also enroll for residence. Registration for residence should be done within the semester. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-03 | A student who does not intend to enroll for a valid reason but still has remaining units is not allowed to enroll in Residency, but files a leave of absence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-04 | For the comprehensive examination the Academic Coordinator does the initial evaluation and the student is advised to enroll in Residency in Comprehensive Examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| RES-05 | Master's program: candidacy must be attained within three academic years from the date of admission and all requirements completed within five academic years. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RES-06 | Master's program: a student who cannot comply within five years is allowed a maximum two-year extension but must enroll in a graded six-unit refresher course related to the area of specialization. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RES-07 | A master's program must be finished within a maximum of seven academic years including leave of absence; a student who cannot finish within seven years must re-enroll all the courses taken and earn new credits. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-08 | Doctorate program: candidacy within five academic years from admission; all requirements including the dissertation within seven academic years. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-09 | Doctorate program: beyond seven years a maximum two-year extension with a graded six-unit refresher course; the program must be finished within nine academic years including leave of absence, after which the student must re-enroll all courses taken and earn credit units. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-10 | Thesis or project paper writing must be finished within two years from the title or proposal defense; dissertation writing within three years from the title defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| RES-11 | The platform records each residency enrollment with its purpose (thesis or dissertation work, practicum or internship, comprehensive examination, awaiting publication) and marks a purpose as unsupported if the student's record does not match it; staff may still record it with notes that explain the exception. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): residency policy review | Proposed |

#### 4.9.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- A student past five (master's) or seven (doctorate) years: extension with a graded six-unit refresher course is possible up to seven or nine years (RES-06, RES-09).
- A student past seven (master's) or nine (doctorate) years: all courses must be re-enrolled (RES-07, RES-09).
- A student with units remaining who will not enroll: a leave of absence, not residency (RES-03).
- The Handbook does not state a fee for residency enrollment or a limit on the number of residency semesters (OQ-15).

#### 4.9.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-10 Application for Residency Enrollment. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Residency enrollment and its purpose | Graduate School record; AIMS |
| Years in program and residence classification | Graduate School record (residency watchlist) |

#### 4.9.8 How the platform supports this process

GS staff or an Academic Coordinator record a residency enrollment; it is not started by the student. The purpose (thesis or dissertation work, practicum or internship, comprehensive examination, awaiting publication) comes from the student's stage, and the review runs by itself when the record is made. The student must have no subject currently enrolled; the purpose must fit the student's record (the comprehensive examination needs the Comprehensive Exam stage, publication needs the writing, final defense or completed stage, thesis or practicum work needs no missing subjects); and the record must not be a substitute for a leave. A record that fails the review can be saved only with staff notes that explain the exception. A student who is AWOL, on leave, withdrawn or completed is blocked. A student can have one active residency enrollment per semester, and the number of residency semesters is not limited. The platform counts years in the program in academic years (the entry academic year is year 1 and the count steps up each June) and never reduces the count for a leave. It classifies each student against the register (master's: 5 normal and 7 absolute years; doctorate: 7 and 9; an extension of 2 years with a graded 6-unit refresher) and lists students on a watchlist as Within, Approaching or Exceeded.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform no longer pauses the residency clock during a leave of absence: it follows the Handbook, where maximum residence includes leave (RES-07, RES-09; register value residency.includes_loa). The year count starts from the entry academic year and not from the date of admission, so it can differ from the Handbook's count by up to a year (OQ-16). With the Handbook's two-year extension, the year after the normal period is already within one year of the absolute limit, so the watchlist shows Approaching and a separate state for a student past the normal period does not appear. The purpose of completing an INC (RES-02) is not among the platform's purposes. The platform creates a task for the refresher course when the Dean approves an extension but does not track the course or approve the extension (RES-06), and it does not track candidacy dates (RES-05, RES-08) or the research writing limits (RES-10); the register shows these as documented only. The fee and the number of residency semesters are open (OQ-15).

### 4.10 Comprehensive examination

#### 4.10.1 Purpose

To state who may take the comprehensive examination, how to apply, how it is scored, and what happens after a failure.

#### 4.10.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Academic Coordinator (master's) or College Dean (doctorate)
- Graduate Program Secretary
- Business Office (external)
- Graduate School Dean

#### 4.10.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** A student has passed all academic requirements and has been evaluated by the Academic Coordinator (master's) or the Dean (doctorate).

**Preconditions.**

- Master's: all academic requirements passed and evaluation by the Graduate School Academic Coordinator.
- Non-thesis track: the integrating courses (feasibility study, project study, case study, applied research, or action research or practicum) are completed.
- Doctorate: evaluation by the College Dean.

#### 4.10.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Academic Coordinator | Makes an initial evaluation of the student's academic courses and advises the student to enroll in Residency in Comprehensive Examination. | Initial evaluation | — |
| 2 | Student | Files the application with the Graduate Program Secretary. | Application for the comprehensive examination | At least two weeks before the scheduled examination |
| 3 | Student | Pays the examination fee at the Business Office and presents the official receipt to the secretary before taking the exam. | Official receipt | Before the exam |
| 4 | Student (if withdrawing) | Informs the Graduate Program in writing, with the reason. | Written withdrawal of application | At least three working days before the examination |
| 5 | Student | Takes the examination: two days for a master's, three for a doctorate, not more than eight hours per day. | Examination | As scheduled |
| 6 | Graduate School | Scores the examination; a retake applies to failed courses. | Score per course | — |
| 7 | Student / Dean | After passing, confers with the Dean for a thesis or dissertation adviser; the student is considered advanced to candidacy. | Advancement to candidacy | — |

#### 4.10.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| CEX-01 | The comprehensive examinations cover all basic or core courses, fields of concentration, and electives or cognates. They are given for two days for a master's and three days for a doctorate, with two-day intervals between examinations or consecutively as arranged, and last a maximum of eight hours per day. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-02 | Master's scope: first day basic courses and electives or cognates; second day major courses. Doctorate scope: first day basic courses and electives or cognates; second and third days major courses. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-03 | Master's students who have passed all academic requirements and been evaluated by their Graduate School Academic Coordinators may file an application; doctorate students evaluated by their College Deans may likewise file. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-04 | Non-thesis students must have completed the integrating courses (feasibility study, project study, case study or applied research in the major field for MBA; action research or practicum in the major field for Education). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-05 | The application is filed with the Graduate Program Secretary at least two weeks before the scheduled examination; an examinee who withdraws the application informs the Graduate Program in writing with the reason at least three working days before the examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 56-57 | Official |
| CEX-06 | The examination fee is P1,100 for a master's and P2,500 for a doctorate, paid to the Business Office; the official receipt attached to the application is presented to the secretary before the exam. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-07 | The fee is forfeited if the examinee fails to appear during the examination dates or fails to inform the Graduate Program Services Office by a written note. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-08 | A student must get a minimum score of 7.00 points (70%) in all courses to pass. A student below 7.00 may take a first retake in the failed courses within the residency enrollment and is allowed two retakes within the semester. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-09 | A student who fails the second retake takes a refresher course on the failed courses the following semester and may take the examination for the third and last time in the following residency enrollment; failure the fourth time means disqualification from the program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-10 | A student who passes the comprehensive examination confers with the Dean for a thesis or dissertation adviser and is considered advanced to candidacy. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-11 | Passing the comprehensive examination is a graduation requirement; for special academic honors the examination must be passed with no retake. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 55, 77 | Official |
| CEX-12 | The monitoring workbook records the comprehensive examination in its own column. | Group working notes, Monitoring workbook, COMPRE column | Practice |
| CEX-13 | The platform keeps all research stages from the title defense onward locked until the comprehensive examination status is Passed and all curriculum subjects are complete; the Graduate School can switch the examination requirement off in the register. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): comprehensive exam eligibility | Proposed |

#### 4.10.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- The Handbook's sentence on a student who fails to take the examination on the scheduled dates is cut off in the copy supplied (Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57); the consequence is an open question (OQ-18).
- A student who fails a retake takes a refresher course the next semester (CEX-09).
- A master's student is evaluated by the Academic Coordinator but a doctorate student by the College Dean; the office may prefer the Graduate School Dean (OQ-18).

#### 4.10.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-14 Application for Comprehensive Examination; GS-F-15 Comprehensive Examination Result Sheet. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Application, official receipt and score per course | Graduate Program Secretary; Academic Coordinator |
| Comprehensive examination result | Monitoring workbook (COMPRE column) |

#### 4.10.8 How the platform supports this process

The platform works out whether a student is eligible for the comprehensive examination and does not let a user mark it. For the MBA and the Doctor of Psychology curricula, which it holds in full, eligibility is judged by the units completed in each category; for other programs every curriculum subject must be complete. In both cases no subject may be failed. The result of the examination (Not Taken or Passed) is not entered in the portal; it reaches the student's record only from the COMPRE column of the monitoring workbook. The platform does not record the application, the fee, the score per course, retakes or the refresher course. Until the status is Passed and the curriculum is complete, all research stages from the title defense onward are locked (register rule research.comprehensive_exam_before_research). The Graduate School can switch that rule off in the register, in which case research opens when the student is eligible.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** Graduate School staff record the examination result (Passed or Failed, the exam date and a reason) on the student record; the platform keeps who recorded it and when, marks it as a manual entry and writes it to the activity log (I-335). The platform blocks the research steps until the examination is passed (CEX-13), which the Research Protocol does not state and the Handbook states only as the step before the student confers with the Dean for an adviser (p. 57). The Handbook also says an adviser may be assigned while the student is still taking courses (ADV-08); the sequence relative to the title defense is an open question (OQ-17). The evaluation by the Academic Coordinator or College Dean (CEX-03), the application, fee, scoring and retakes (CEX-05 to CEX-09) and the honors condition (CEX-11) are not modelled and are left to people.

### 4.11 Title defense

#### 4.11.1 Purpose

To have the student's three proposed research titles deliberated by a panel, so that one title is approved for the project paper, thesis or dissertation.

#### 4.11.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Academic Coordinator
- Research Coordinator (RC)
- GS Research Committee
- GS Office (scheduling)
- Panel chair and panel members

#### 4.11.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** The student is ready to propose a research title.

**Preconditions.**

- The student has three concept papers.
- The student applies at least two weeks before the defense (TTL-01).
- Platform only: the comprehensive examination has been passed (see CEX-13 and OQ-17).

#### 4.11.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Student | Accomplishes Form 1, Application for Title Defense, and emails Form 1 and the three concept papers to the Academic Coordinator. | Form 1; 3 concept papers | At least 2 weeks before the scheduled defense |
| 2 | Academic Coordinator | Endorses Form 1 and the three concept papers to the Research Coordinator at the GS mailbox by e-signature (instead of signing Form 1), and recommends a set of panel members. | Form 1 with AC e-signature | — |
| 3 | Research Coordinator | Nominates the panel members as recommended by the Academic Coordinator, after deliberation with the GS Research Committee. | Panel nomination | — |
| 4 | GS Office | Coordinates with the panel members about the proposed schedule of the title defense. | Schedule | — |
| 5 | Research Coordinator | Informs the nominated panel members and the student of the date and time, and sends the three concept papers and Form 2 to the panel. | Form 2 (blank); concept papers | — |
| 6 | Panel chair | Prepares and emails the online details (meeting ID and password, or meeting link) to panel and student before the defense, and hosts the defense. | Meeting details | Before the defense |
| 7 | Panel | Deliberates the three titles without the student. The chair then admits the student, who takes note of the comments. The chair may record the defense with everyone's consent. | Comments | At the defense |
| 8 | Panel members | Decide the title to be pursued; each accomplishes Form 2 with the result and other information. | Form 2 | After the defense |
| 9 | Panel chair | Emails the accomplished Form 2 to the Research Coordinator and the student. | Form 2 | Within the first working day after the defense |
| 10 | Student | If the titles were disapproved, presents a new set of three titles and repeats the protocol. Settles the defense fees with the panel and emails the screenshot or deposit slip to the GS mailbox with the subject LAST NAME_type of defense_receipt. | New titles; payment proof | Within the first working day after the defense |

#### 4.11.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| TTL-01 | The application for title defense (Form 1 and three concept papers to the Academic Coordinator) must be made at least two weeks before the scheduled defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 2 note | Official |
| TTL-02 | The Research Coordinator nominates the panel members as recommended by the Academic Coordinator, after deliberation with the GS Research Committee. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-03 | Panel for a project paper: one panel chair and two members (one content specialist and one method specialist). Thesis or bridging thesis: one chair and three members (one content specialist, one method specialist and one external panel). Dissertation: one chair and four members (two content specialists, one method specialist and one external panel). The chair and the content and method specialists are University-affiliated; the external panel is a content or industry expert who takes part from the proposal defense onwards. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-04 | The panel members deliberate the three submitted titles without the presence of the student; the chair then admits the student. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part II | Official |
| TTL-05 | The panel chair may record the defense with the consent of everyone in the meeting room and furnish the student the link of the recording. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60 | Official |
| TTL-06 | The panel chair emails the accomplished Form 2 to the Research Coordinator and the student within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 2 | Official |
| TTL-07 | If the proposed titles are disapproved, the student presents a new set of three titles and follows the same protocol; the decision is reflected in Form 2. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 3 | Official |
| TTL-08 | The student asks the panel about their preferred mode of payment, settles the title defense fees and emails the proof to the GS mailbox within the first working day after the defense, with the subject line LAST NAME_type of defense_receipt. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-09 | Thesis or project paper writing must be finished within two years from the title or proposal defense; for the thesis the student has one year from the title defense to submit and defend a proposal. Dissertation writing must be finished within three years from the title defense, with one year to submit and defend a proposal. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| TTL-10 | If the approved title is still applicable, the same concept paper may be presented for re-defense; otherwise the student changes the research title or topic and goes through the title defense again. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| TTL-11 | GS staff check that Form 1, three concept papers and the Academic Coordinator's endorsement are present before the Research Coordinator nominates a panel. The check is for completeness only; in the platform the endorsement is refused unless Form 1 and all three papers are uploaded, and panel matching opens only after it. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 42; Group working notes, transactions list | Proposed |
| TTL-12 | The platform does not schedule a title defense, nominate a panel or approve a title by itself; panel candidates are suggested from the uploaded concept papers, faculty specialization, availability and workload, and staff may choose any active faculty member. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 44 | Proposed |

#### 4.11.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- All three titles disapproved: the student presents three new titles and repeats the process (TTL-07).
- Student wants to keep the approved title after a long delay: the same concept paper may be re-presented if still applicable (TTL-10).
- Student abroad: the defense is online; the chair sends the login details (Research Protocol, Proposal Defense, Part I, states this for the proposal defense; the title defense is held on the chosen platform).
- Panel members cannot agree on a title: the Protocol does not say what happens.
- Fee tables are blank in the copy of the Protocol supplied; amounts are not known (OQ-27).

#### 4.11.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-16 Application for Title Defense; GS-F-17 Panel Nomination and Invitation Reply Slip; GS-F-18 Report of Title Defense. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Form 1 and the three concept papers | Research Coordinator; GS mailbox |
| Form 2, Report of Title Defense (approved title) | Research Coordinator; research monitoring workbook (approved title, schedule) |
| Fee payment proof | GS mailbox |

#### 4.11.8 How the platform supports this process

The student uploads Form 1 and exactly three concept-paper PDFs in the platform. The Academic Coordinator endorses them with a drawn signature; the endorsement is refused unless Form 1 and all three papers are present, and uploading the papers again withdraws it. Panel matching opens only after that endorsement. The platform ranks faculty by comparing the uploaded papers with the faculty expertise records, by availability and by current panel load, and leaves out the adviser. The Research Coordinator or GS staff finalise the panel and may choose any active faculty member, but the platform checks the size of the panel, its roles and that its members are distinct before it accepts the panel, and a change to an existing panel needs a recorded reason. The schedule is booked by staff (see 4.16). The panel chair assigned to the defense enters the verdict in the Faculty Portal, or Graduate School staff or the Research Coordinator record it for the panel from the Research Gate with a reason, only after the booked start time. The verdicts are Passed; Passed with minor revisions; Provisional pass, revisions or re-defense; Deferred; Failed. A pass may name the selected title, which becomes the study title. Deferred and Failed reopen the stage's requirements for the student before another booking.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform now follows the Handbook and the Protocol on the points where the earlier build had differed. A title defense needs 14 days' notice, counted from the Academic Coordinator's endorsement of Form 1 (TTL-01); staff cannot proceed inside that time (OQ-43). A project paper panel is the chair and two members (content and method) with no external member; the external member joins thesis and dissertation panels from the proposal defense (TTL-03, OQ-42). The platform also requires the comprehensive examination to be Passed, and every curriculum subject complete, before any title-defense step: the Handbook places the examination before the adviser and candidacy (p. 57), the Research Protocol does not state it (CEX-13, OQ-17). Not in the platform: the deliberation of the GS Research Committee on the panel (TTL-02), the paper Form 2 and the one-working-day deadline for it (TTL-06), the fee proof (TTL-08, OQ-27), and the approval of the same concept paper for a re-defense (TTL-10). The writing limits of TTL-09 are shown as information only.

### 4.12 Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser

#### 4.12.1 Purpose

To appoint a research adviser for a student, to set the advising timetable and contract, and to handle the one allowed change of title and change of adviser.

#### 4.12.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Academic Coordinator
- Research Coordinator (RC)
- Associate Dean
- Graduate School Dean
- Adviser and proposed adviser
- Panel chair and panel members (change of title)

#### 4.12.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** The student has an approved title or, earlier, wants an adviser while still taking courses.

**Preconditions.**

- The proposed adviser is a faculty of the University whose program is aligned with the student's program.
- The proposed adviser is within the advisee limit.

#### 4.12.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Student | Accomplishes Form 3, Application for Designation of Research Adviser, and emails it to the Academic Coordinator. | Form 3 | — |
| 2 | Academic Coordinator | Writes an email or letter to the Research Coordinator at the GS mailbox "noting" the application. The Dean's e-signature is not required at this point. | AC note | — |
| 3 | Research Coordinator | Emails Form 3 to the Dean for deliberation and appropriate action. Once the adviser is decided, the Research Coordinator, the Associate Dean and the Dean affix their e-signatures. | Form 3 signed by RC, AD, Dean | — |
| 4 | Research Coordinator | Emails Form 3.1, Appointment for Research Adviser, to the student and the appointed adviser. | Form 3.1 | — |
| 5 | Student / adviser | Email back the accomplished Form 3.1 to the Research Coordinator. | Form 3.1 (accomplished) | — |
| 6 | Student and adviser | Accomplish Form 3.2, Research Timetable, and Form 3.3, Thesis/Dissertation Advising Contract, and the student emails them to the Research Coordinator. | Forms 3.2, 3.3 | Within 5 days of receipt of Form 3.1 |
| 7 | Student (change of title) | Emails Form 3.4 and the new concept paper to the adviser, who endorses the request to the panel (copy to the RC). The panel deliberates and the chair emails the decision to the student, adviser and RC. | Form 3.4; new concept paper | Before the proposal defense application |
| 8 | Student (change of adviser) | Accomplishes Form 3.1.1 signed by the current and the new adviser and emails it to the Research Coordinator. The RC, Associate Dean and Dean deliberate; if approved, a new Form 3.1 is prepared. | Form 3.1.1; new Form 3.1 | Before the proposal defense |

#### 4.12.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| ADV-01 | The research adviser must be affiliated with the University and the degree program must be aligned with the student's program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, step 1 | Official |
| ADV-02 | Protocol: the research adviser must have a PhD degree for PhD students and at least PhD units for MA students (CHED CMO 15), and should have a maximum of five active advisees for the current academic year. | Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, step 1 note | Official |
| ADV-03 | Handbook: the adviser shall be a faculty of the Graduate School and a holder of a doctoral degree aligned with the discipline, with at least one publication in a refereed journal or publicly recognized creative or technology outputs (CMO 15 s. 2019). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-04 | In the exigency of service a faculty member may have more than five advisees with the approval of the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-05 | The student may express a preference, but the Dean, the Associate Dean and the Research Coordinator make the final decision based on the degree, expertise, research work, number of advisees (maximum five per semester) and total workload of the faculty member. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-06 | The choice of adviser gives primary importance to the student's choice with the approval of the program adviser and the Dean; the Dean appoints the adviser on the basis of expertise in the student's topic following the Research Protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-07 | Faculty of other universities may serve as adviser in some circumstances if they are adjunct faculty of the Graduate School. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-08 | The assignment of an adviser may be done while the student is still taking academic courses. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-09 | Form 3 is emailed by the student to the Academic Coordinator, who notes it to the Research Coordinator; the Dean's e-signature is not required at that point; the Research Coordinator emails Form 3 to the Dean; the Research Coordinator, the Associate Dean and the Dean sign; the Research Coordinator emails Form 3.1 to the student and adviser, who email the accomplished form back. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, steps 2-5 | Official |
| ADV-10 | The advisee and adviser accomplish Form 3.2 and Form 3.3; the student emails them to the Research Coordinator within five days of receipt of Form 3.1. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 61-62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part II | Official |
| ADV-11 | The adviser is bound only to advising and shall not be selected as editor of the student or to any other appointment related to the conduct of the study. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-12 | The adviser monitors the research according to the timetable in Form 3.2, reviews submitted proposals, questionnaires and chapters, gives technical and moral guidance, conducts a mock defense when necessary, attends the defense of the advisee, attends orientations set by the Graduate School, and adheres to the Research Protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-13 | A student may make only one request to change the title of the study, before the proposal defense application: Form 3.4 and the new concept paper go to the adviser, who endorses the request to the panel; the panel deliberates and the chair emails the decision. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Change of Title of the Study | Official |
| ADV-14 | A student may make only one request to change the research adviser, before the proposal defense, for justifiable reasons: Form 3.1.1 signed by the current and new adviser, deliberated by the Research Coordinator, Associate Dean and Dean; then a new Form 3.1. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Request for Change of Research Adviser | Official |
| ADV-15 | An adviser designated before the effectivity of his or her retirement may continue to serve until the final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-16 | The student needs to propose three faculty as adviser options. | Consultation 21 July 2026, Associate Dean's description | Practice |
| ADV-17 | Each nominated faculty member may accept or decline the nomination (the Associate Dean suggested that the platform should offer this; it does not yet); the student and the Graduate School then choose among those who accept. | Consultation 21 July 2026, Associate Dean's suggestion | Proposed |
| ADV-18 | Adviser-dependent steps (adviser signature, endorsement, document review, availability in scheduling) cannot be completed until an adviser is designated. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 43 | Proposed |

#### 4.12.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- The proposed adviser is over the advisee limit: the Dean may approve more than five in the exigency of service (ADV-04); the Handbook says five per semester and the Protocol five active advisees per academic year (OQ-20).
- The student wants a second change of title or adviser: the Protocol allows only one of each and only before the proposal defense (ADV-13, ADV-14).
- The adviser is unavailable or leaves the University: not stated in the sources, apart from retirement (ADV-15).

#### 4.12.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-19 Application for Designation of Research Adviser; GS-F-20 Appointment for Research Adviser; GS-F-21 Request for Change of Research Adviser; GS-F-22 Research Timetable; GS-F-23 Thesis / Dissertation Advising Contract; GS-F-24 Request for Change of Title of the Study. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Forms 3 and 3.1 with e-signatures | Research Coordinator; research monitoring workbook (research adviser) |
| Forms 3.2 and 3.3 | Research Coordinator |
| Forms 3.4 and 3.1.1 and the decisions | Research Coordinator |

#### 4.12.8 How the platform supports this process

The platform does not run the adviser designation process. Form 3 and its companion forms (3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4 and 3.1.1), the nomination of three faculty, the signatures of the Research Coordinator, the Associate Dean and the Dean, and the changes of title and of adviser all happen outside it. The adviser appears in the platform as a name on the student's record, set by GS staff when the monitoring record is created or corrected (a correction needs a recorded reason) and linked to the adviser's faculty login. From then on the platform uses the adviser in four ways. The adviser signs on screen the exact manuscript that the student uploaded, the Form 4 endorsements and the final manuscript; confirms minor revisions; is counted in the availability needed to book a defense; and can never sit on the same student's panel. Without the adviser's signature the proposal and final stages cannot be completed, although staff can still book a defense with a recorded reason.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform does not count an adviser's advisees, does not check the adviser's qualifications (CMO 15, doctoral degree, publication) and does not enforce the one-change limits for title and adviser. The register holds the five-advisee limit as a documented rule only (ADV-02, ADV-04, ADV-05, ADV-13, ADV-14). Who finally approves an adviser, and the advisee limit, differ between the Handbook and the Protocol (OQ-19, OQ-20). The three-nominee practice and the step in which each faculty member accepts or declines (ADV-16, ADV-17) are not built (OQ-21).

### 4.13 Proposal defense and ethics review

#### 4.13.1 Purpose

To have the student's research proposal examined by a panel and, after revisions, to obtain the technical review certificate and ethics clearance that precede the final defense.

#### 4.13.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Statistician
- Research adviser
- Research Coordinator
- GS Research Committee
- Panel chair and panel members (including the external panel)
- Research ethics office
- GS Office

#### 4.13.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** The student has an adviser and a proposal paper that complies with the Graduate School's research paper guidelines.

**Preconditions.**

- One year from the title defense has not passed (TTL-09).
- The student has consulted a statistician (quantitative studies).
- An adviser is designated (ADV-18).

#### 4.13.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Student | Consults a statistician (quantitative studies) and obtains the accomplished Form 4.1, Statistical Consultation Form. Form 4.1 is not required for qualitative studies. | Form 4.1 | Before the proposal defense |
| 2 | Student | Emails the adviser the proposal paper and Form 4.1. | Proposal paper; Form 4.1 | — |
| 3 | Research adviser | Reviews the manuscript and, once it complies with the Graduate School's research paper guidelines, accomplishes Form 4, Endorsement for Proposal/Final Defense, with e-signature. | Form 4 | At least 2 weeks before the date of the defense |
| 4 | Student (optional) | Submits Form 4.2, Co-authorship Information for Publication, if the student will have a co-author. | Form 4.2 | — |
| 5 | Research adviser | Recommends the preferred external panel, whose expertise is aligned with the student's. The recommendation is subject to the approval of the GS Research Committee (Protocol) or the GS office (Handbook). | Recommendation | — |
| 6 | Student and adviser | Agree with the panel members on the date and time, indicate the agreed schedule in Form 4, and give the panel the manuscript and Form 4.1. | Form 4 (schedule) | Panel has two weeks to review before the defense |
| 7 | Research adviser | Emails Form 4, the proposal paper and Form 4.1 to the Research Coordinator. | Form 4 | — |
| 8 | Research Coordinator | Emails the manuscript, Form 4.1 and Form 5, Comments Sheet for Proposal Defense, to the panel, adviser and student. | Form 5 (blank) | — |
| 9 | Panel chair | Coordinates with the GS Office for the venue (or prepares online details, including for students abroad) and tells student, adviser and panel. | Venue or online details | Before the defense |
| 10 | Panel | Deliberates first without student and adviser; then the chair admits both. Before the presentation, the chair asks whether the student has a study grant and a plan for a co-author. The adviser records the comments. After the presentation and questions, the panel deliberates and announces the result. If major revisions are recommended, a re-defense is advised. | Form 5 | At the defense |
| 11 | Each panel member / panel chair | Each member accomplishes Form 5 and emails it to the chair; the chair emails the consolidated Form 5 to the student, adviser and Research Coordinator. | Consolidated Form 5 | Within the first working day after the defense |
| 12 | Student / adviser / panel | The student revises; the adviser endorses the revised manuscript and Form 5 to the panel for approval and the release of Form 5.1, Technical Review Certificate; the chair emails Form 5.1 to the student and adviser with copy to the Research Coordinator. | Form 5.1 | — |
| 13 | Student | Accomplishes Form 5.2, Application for Ethics Review, and emails it with the revised manuscript and Form 5.1 to the research ethics office. | Form 5.2 | Within one month after the proposal defense |
| 14 | Student | Settles the panel fees and emails the proof to the GS mailbox. | Payment proof | Within the first working day after the defense |

#### 4.13.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PRP-01 | Before the proposal defense the student must have consulted a statistician for a quantitative study, with Form 4.1 accomplished by the statistician. Form 4.1 is not required for qualitative studies. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (statistical consultation) | Official |
| PRP-02 | The research adviser accomplishes Form 4 with e-signature once the paper complies with the research paper guidelines set by the Graduate School Office; this must be done at least two weeks before the date of the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (Form 4 endorsement and note) | Official |
| PRP-03 | The panel members are given two weeks to review the paper before the scheduled proposal defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (panel review period) | Official |
| PRP-04 | The adviser's recommendation of an external panel is subject to the approval of the GS Research Committee (Dean, Associate Dean, Academic Coordinator, Research Coordinator) according to the Protocol, and to the approval of the GS office according to the Handbook. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (external panel recommendation) | Official |
| PRP-05 | For co-authorship, the student must have the major part and contribute at least 80% during the conduct of the study and must adhere to the contribution of work in Form 4.2; co-authorship or study grant agreements follow the institutional guidelines of the Center for Research and Engagement. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part II (co-authorship note) | Official |
| PRP-06 | The panel deliberates the paper first without the student and the adviser; if major revisions are recommended the student is advised to do a re-defense following the same protocol, reflected in Form 5. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part II | Official |
| PRP-07 | The panel chair emails the consolidated Form 5 to the student, the adviser and the Research Coordinator within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (consolidated Form 5) | Official |
| PRP-08 | The application for ethics review (Form 5.2 with the revised manuscript and Form 5.1) is made within one month after the proposal defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (ethics review note) | Official |
| PRP-09 | The student settles payment to the preferred method of the panel members and emails the proof to the GS mailbox within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (payment) | Official |
| PRP-10 | A student who has used a change of title or a change of adviser must have done so before the proposal defense application (ADV-13, ADV-14). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62 | Official |
| PRP-11 | Ethics clearance is needed before the closed-door final defense: the adviser emails Form 4, the Ethics Clearance and the manuscript to the Research Coordinator. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (Form 4 submission) | Official |
| PRP-12 | The Research Coordinator records ethics clearance as received; the office checks that the document is present, not its content. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 42 | Proposed |
| PRP-13 | The platform does not complete the proposal stage without the adviser's signature on the manuscript and on Form 4, and it asks every student for Form 4.1 (it does not distinguish quantitative from qualitative studies). | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 43; Platform (current build) | Proposed |

#### 4.13.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- Major revisions: the student is advised to re-defend and follows the same protocol (PRP-06).
- Qualitative study: Form 4.1 is not required (PRP-01).
- Student abroad: the panel chair recommends an online deliberation and sends the login details (Research Protocol, Proposal Defense, Part I).
- The external panel member: from the proposal defense onward (TTL-03); approval route differs (OQ-22).
- Panel fee amounts are not known (OQ-27).

#### 4.13.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-25 Statistical Consultation Form; GS-F-26 Co-authorship Information for Publication; GS-F-27 Endorsement for Proposal / Final Defense; GS-F-29 Comments Sheet for Proposal Defense; GS-F-30 Technical Review Certificate; GS-F-31 Application for Ethics Review. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Forms 4, 4.1, 4.2 and the manuscript | Research Coordinator; research monitoring workbook (endorsement and schedule of proposal) |
| Form 5, consolidated comments sheet | Research Coordinator; student; adviser |
| Form 5.1 Technical Review Certificate; Form 5.2; ethics clearance | Research Coordinator; research ethics office; research monitoring workbook (technical clearance, ethics submission, ethics clearance) |

#### 4.13.8 How the platform supports this process

The student uploads the proposal manuscript, Form 4, Form 4.1 and the endorsement. Each needs the adviser's on-screen signature on that exact file, and a replaced file returns to the adviser's queue. The proposal stage lists ten items: the manuscript, the panel, the Form 4 endorsement, Form 4.1 (or the qualitative exemption), the adviser's signature, the schedule, the result, Form 5.1, the ethics clearance and the ethics status record. Form 4.1 is asked of every student; the platform does not ask whether the study is quantitative or qualitative. The panel is the full panel, including the external member for a thesis or dissertation; the platform does not check that the external member is from outside the University or that the GS Research Committee approved. Booking follows 4.16, with a 14-day notice that staff can override with a reason. The chair, or staff for the panel, records one of five outcomes (Passed; Passed with minor revisions; Provisional pass, revisions or re-defense, with the follow-up picked; Deferred; Failed), only at or after the booked time. Form 5.1 and the ethics items stay locked until the proposal is passed and its revisions are confirmed. The Research Coordinator records ethics as Cleared (this needs a date and Forms 5.1 and 5.2 uploaded), Returned or Not cleared. The proposal stage completes, and the final stage opens, only when ethics is Cleared.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform checks that documents are present and signed, not that they are valid. The month after the proposal defense for the ethics application (PRP-08) is a dated task of 30 days, not a block. It has no step for Form 4.2 and the co-author contribution (PRP-05). The two-week review period (PRP-03) is a warning that can be overridden. The approval of the external panel member (PRP-04, OQ-22), the statistician's consultation itself and the payment proof (PRP-09, OQ-27) are left to people. Ethics clearance before the final defense (PRP-11) is enforced.

### 4.14 Final defense (closed-door and public)

#### 4.14.1 Purpose

To have the completed thesis or dissertation examined in a closed-door final defense and, for students admitted from August 2020, in a public final defense.

#### 4.14.2 Who is involved

- Student
- Research adviser
- Research Coordinator
- Panel chair and panel members
- GS Office
- Associate Dean and Dean (copied on the public announcement)

#### 4.14.3 Trigger and preconditions

**Trigger.** The student has completed the study, has ethics clearance, and the adviser endorses the final manuscript.

**Preconditions.**

- Ethics clearance.
- The manuscript has been given to the panel members before the schedule is set (FIN-05).
- Students admitted before August 2020 do not do the public final defense (FIN-16).

#### 4.14.4 Procedure

| Step | Who | What they do | Form / record | Time limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Student | Tells the adviser, with the final manuscript, that the student is ready for the closed-door final defense. | Final manuscript | — |
| 2 | Research adviser | Reviews the manuscript and accomplishes Form 4 with e-signature. | Form 4 | — |
| 3 | Student and adviser | Arrange the target date with the panel; the manuscript must already be with the panel. The adviser emails Form 4, the Ethics Clearance and the manuscript to the Research Coordinator. | Form 4; ethics clearance | At least 2 weeks before the defense; panel has 2 weeks to review (14-Day Rule) |
| 4 | Research Coordinator | Emails Form 6, Comments Sheet for Final Defense, and Form 7, Evaluation Sheet for Final Defense, to the panel. | Forms 6, 7 (blank) | — |
| 5 | Panel chair | Reserves the room through the GS Office, emails the venue, and hosts the defense. The same panel takes part. | Venue | Before the defense |
| 6 | Panel | Discusses the manuscript without student and adviser; the student presents; the adviser records comments; the panel deliberates and announces pass, pass with minor revisions, or provisional pass with major revisions (subject to re-defense). | Result | At the defense |
| 7 | Adviser | If the student has a provisional pass and no re-defense is recommended, accomplishes Form 8 and emails it to the GS Research Coordinator. | Form 8 | — |
| 8 | Panel members / panel chair | Each member accomplishes Form 6 (to the chair) and Form 7 (to the Research Coordinator); the chair emails all Forms 6 and the consolidated comments to the Research Coordinator, adviser and student. | Forms 6, 7 | Within the first working day after the defense |
| 9 | Student | Settles the panel fees and emails the proof to the GS mailbox. | Payment proof | Within the first working day |
| 10 | Student (admitted August 2020 onward) | Is given two to four weeks to prepare and apply for the public final defense; the adviser reviews compliance (Form 6 with Action Taken and page numbers) and the student and adviser agree a date with the panel. | Form 6 (Action Taken; page numbers) | 2 to 4 weeks |
| 11 | Research adviser | Emails Form 4.3, Endorsement for Public Defense, fully accomplished with the panel members' e-signatures and the date they received the revised manuscript, to the Research Coordinator. | Form 4.3 | At least 2 weeks before the defense; application 5 days before for posting (conflict, OQ-23) |
| 12 | GS Office / Research Coordinator | Reserves the venue; the Research Coordinator prepares the announcement, sends it to adviser, student, panel and, in copy, to the Associate Dean and Dean, and posts it on the Graduate School official Facebook page with a pre-registration link; campus entry is processed with the GS Office. | Public announcement | — |
| 13 | Panel chair | Gives a brief orientation; the student presents for 30 minutes; questions follow (the audience may ask); the panel deliberates; the chair announces the result and the audience leaves; the panel may then emphasize comments not yet addressed. | Forms 6 and 7 | At the defense |

#### 4.14.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| FIN-01 | Final decisions are pass, pass with minor revisions, and provisional pass with major revisions (subject for re-defense). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-02 | The adviser emails Form 4, the Ethics Clearance and the manuscript to the Research Coordinator at least two weeks before the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (Form 4 submission) | Official |
| FIN-03 | 14-Day Rule: "At least fourteen (14) days, inclusive of weekends, from the date the panel members of the oral defense received their copies of the thesis/dissertation to the date of the oral defense." | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I note | Official |
| FIN-04 | The same set of panel members takes part in the closed-door final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (same panel) | Official |
| FIN-05 | The panel members must not agree with the proposed schedule without the revised manuscript; the manuscript must already have been given to the panel before the date is set. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I note | Official |
| FIN-06 | If a provisional pass with major revisions is given and the panel recommends a re-defense, the student follows the same protocol for the final defense afterward; the same amount of fees applies to the re-defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II note | Official |
| FIN-07 | If a provisional pass with major revisions is given but no re-defense is recommended, the student complies with the corrections; once the revised manuscript is given to the panel for approval the 14-Day Rule applies; the adviser accomplishes Form 8 and emails it to the GS Research Coordinator. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-08 | A student who fails to meet the recommendations of all the panel members at a given time is advised to re-defend following the same protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-09 | A provisional pass with major revisions must be reflected in Form 6 and agree with the score in Form 7: for a thesis the score is below 85; for a dissertation the score is below 90. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II note | Official |
| FIN-10 | Each panel member accomplishes Form 6 and emails it to the panel chair, and accomplishes Form 7 and emails it to the Research Coordinator, within the first working day after the defense (the Protocol adds that the research adviser also accomplishes Form 7); the chair emails all Forms 6 and the consolidated comments to the Research Coordinator, adviser and student within the same time. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| FIN-11 | For students admitted on August 2020 and onwards, the student is given two to four weeks to prepare and apply for the public final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 71; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part IV | Official |
| FIN-12 | Form 4.3 must be fully accomplished with the e-signature of the panel members and the date the panel received the revised manuscript. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 72-73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (Form 4.3 notes) | Official |
| FIN-13 | Form 6 must state the Action Taken by the student to address each comment and the specific page number where the improvement appears. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 72 | Official |
| FIN-14 | Application for the public final defense must be made 5 days before the preferred schedule for posting purposes; Form 4.3 must be submitted at least two weeks before the date of the defense. (The two periods conflict; see OQ-23.) | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 72-73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (Form 4.3 notes) | Official |
| FIN-15 | The announcement of the public defense is posted on the Graduate School Official Facebook Page with an embedded pre-registration link; the Research Coordinator coordinates campus entry with the GS Office. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (announcement and pre-registration) | Official |
| FIN-16 | Students enrolled (admitted) before August 2020 are not required to do a public final defense; instead, after the closed-door final defense, the student presents at a research conference. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 68-69; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III note | Official |
| FIN-17 | In the public final defense the student has 30 minutes to present; the Handbook adds another 30 minutes for questions and answers, and the audience may ask questions. Panel members must have Form 6 in hand to check compliance. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-18 | Only the panel chair submits the defense verdict and the record of the result. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 48 | Proposed |

#### 4.14.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- Provisional pass with major revisions: re-defense or compliance with Form 8 (FIN-06, FIN-07).
- Panel members disagree on whether the recommendations were met: the student re-defends (FIN-08).
- Public defense audience: the audience leaves the room after the result is announced and the panel may emphasize unaddressed comments in private (Handbook 2022-2023, p. 73).
- Final defense fee amounts are not known (OQ-27). The time available to submit the revised manuscript after a pass with minor revisions is not stated.

#### 4.14.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-F-27 Endorsement for Proposal / Final Defense; GS-F-32 Comments Sheet for Final Defense; GS-F-33 Evaluation Sheet for Final Defense; GS-F-34 Settlement of Provisional Thesis / Dissertation Approval; GS-F-35 Endorsement for Public Final Defense. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| Form 4, Form 4.3 and the manuscript | Research Coordinator; research monitoring workbook (endorsement for final defense, schedule of final defense, schedule of public defense) |
| Forms 6, 7 and 8 | Research Coordinator; student; adviser |
| Public announcement | Graduate School Facebook page; Associate Dean and Dean (copy) |

#### 4.14.8 How the platform supports this process

The adviser signs the Form 4 final endorsement and the final manuscript on screen. The final stage lists four items: the endorsement, the signed manuscript, the confirmed schedule and the result. The panel of the proposal defense carries over to the final defense; a change needs a recorded reason and puts any booking back to Needs Re-confirmation. Booking follows 4.16: 14 days counted from the date the panel received the manuscript, which the system takes from the adviser's endorsement and manuscript upload and shows read-only; the 14 days are a hard rule. The panel chair records one of the six outcomes. After a pass with minor revisions the adviser or the chair confirms the student's revision; after a provisional pass with major revisions and no re-defense the chair confirms; after a re-defense or a fail the evidence is archived, the student resubmits, the adviser signs again and staff book again. The chair may enter the Form 7 score; when one is entered and it is below 85 (project paper, thesis) or 90 (dissertation), the platform refuses Passed and minor revisions. A Failed or Deferred verdict is refused when the entered score is a passing score. The public final defense cannot be booked in the platform: there is no Form 4.3, announcement, pre-registration, panel or verdict step for it.
