| 1† | Registrar or ITS | Supplies an Excel export of identity, enrollment and grades (the group was told the Graduate School can ask ITS for one). The proposed workbook has the sheets Export_Metadata, Students, Enrollment_Subjects, Grades and Subject_Offerings. | AIMS export workbook | Start of each term and after grade posting (proposed) |
| 2† | GS staff | Uploads the Academic Coordinator's student monitoring workbook (.xlsx) at student handoff, and the Academic Coordinator or GS staff upload class lists (.csv or .xlsx with a faculty column) to tag students as enrolled or dropped. The platform checks each file, refuses rows it cannot accept, and keeps the original file. | Uploaded workbook; class list | Each term |
| 3† | Platform | Imports identity, enrollment and grade values as read-only and reports any difference from the Graduate School's record. A difference is corrected in AIMS and a replacement export is imported; it is never overwritten in the platform. | Import result; discrepancy list | — |
| 4† | Academic Coordinator or GS staff | Each working day opens the daily change report and applies each listed change in AIMS (or sends it to the Registrar), then marks it reflected. | Daily change report | Each working day (proposed) |
| 5† | GS staff | Exports the Dean-approved leave and readmission cases to a CSV or Excel list for the Registrar, emails it, and marks the cases Sent to Registrar and later Acknowledged. | Registrar list; export log | After the Dean's approval |
| 6† | Graduate School Dean | Approves the graduation endorsements, exports the endorsed list (CSV) and emails it to the Registrar by the official channel. | Endorsed graduation list | After the batch approval |
| 7† | GS staff | Sends each outbound file with a Records Transmittal (GS-F-44) and files the transmittal and the Registrar's acknowledgement. | GS-F-44; acknowledgement | — |

† This step rests on a rule marked Practice or Proposed in section 4.24.5, or on the group's process model; it is not described in the Handbook or the Research Protocol and is to be confirmed by the Graduate School.

**Known gaps (not built in this version)**

| Feature | Issue | What the platform does now | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Several subjects in one withdrawal request | I-279 | Not built. A request covers one subject; the student files one request for each subject. | Deferred (Handbook p. 49 says "subject/s") |
| Public Final Defense, Form 4.3, Form 8 and proof of research-conference presentation | I-293 | Not built. The platform cannot book or record the public final defense (see 4.14). | Deferred |
| Change of title (Form 3.4) | I-317 | Not built. | Deferred |
| The Academic Coordinator's recommendation of the panel | I-319 | Not built. The panel is matched without this recommendation. | Deferred |
| A separate Form 7 score for each panel member | I-326 | Not built. One score is entered for the defense; a Failed or Deferred verdict refuses a passing score. | Deferred |
| A student page to request a residency enrollment | I-346 | Not built. GS staff or an Academic Coordinator record the residency enrollment (see 4.9). | Deferred |
| Three concept-paper titles at upload, with a pick list at the verdict | I-248 | Not built yet. | Planned |
| Practicum hours verified by the Academic Coordinator | I-252 | Not built yet. The hours are the student's own report. | Planned |

#### 4.24.5 Business rules

| Rule | Rule statement | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEX-01 | AIMS stays an external system and the official record of identity, enrollment and grades. The platform has no live link, no AIMS login and no screen reading of AIMS; only files are exchanged. | Consultation 21 July 2026, the group was told the platform cannot be integrated with AIMS; Platform (current build), AIMS boundary | Proposed |
| DEX-02 | Files come in by upload by GS staff or the Academic Coordinator: the student monitoring workbook (.xlsx or .xlsm) at handoff, and class lists (.csv or .xlsx with a faculty column) for enrollment tagging. The platform validates a file, refuses rows it cannot accept (for example a row for another semester, or for a student who is on leave, AWOL or withdrawn) and never changes grades. | Platform (current build), student handoff and class list import | Proposed |
| DEX-03 | The proposed AIMS export contract (version PROPOSED-1.0, Documents/Sample_Data/AIMS_Export/) is a workbook with the sheets Export_Metadata, Students, Enrollment_Subjects, Grades and Subject_Offerings. STUDENT_NUMBER is the identity key; official values are kept as exported. The exact columns, the exporting office and the cadence are for the AIMS and Registrar owner to confirm (OQ-50). The current build does not yet read this workbook. | Platform (current build), Documents/Sample_Data/AIMS_Export | Proposed |
| DEX-04 | The files from AIMS or the Registrar are supplied at the start of each term, after enrollment closes, and again after grades are posted (proposed). Between those dates the platform sends changes out by the daily change report (DEX-06). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| DEX-05 | Identity, enrollment, grade and completion values imported from AIMS are read-only. If the Graduate School's record differs, the difference is flagged for follow-up and corrected in AIMS, and a replacement export is imported with its history kept. | This manual, section 3.3; Platform (current build), read-only official data | Proposed |
| DEX-06 | Every change made in the platform that the Registrar must apply in AIMS (enrollment or subject change, withdrawal, course adjustment, leave, readmission, AWOL or residency, graduation) appears on a dated daily change report. The Academic Coordinator or GS staff apply it in AIMS and mark it reflected; the platform does not write to AIMS. | Platform (current build), daily change report | Proposed |
| DEX-07 | Only leave and readmission cases that the Dean has approved can be exported to the Registrar list. Each export is logged with the file name, the person and the time, and a case moves from Pending Handoff to Exported - Ready to Send, then to Sent to Registrar and Acknowledged when GS staff record them (see GRD-16, OQ-32). | Platform (current build), leave and readmission Registrar export | Proposed |
| DEX-08 | The graduation list is exported only for endorsements the Dean has approved and for students who are still eligible when the file is made. It is a CSV with batch, student number, name, program, coursework status, research status, endorsement status, the Dean's approval date and remarks, and the Registrar export status, and the Dean emails it manually (GRD-16). | Platform (current build), endorsed graduation list | Proposed |
| DEX-09 | An outbound file holds only the columns the Registrar needs and goes by official University email, never by personal email or chat (PRV-05). | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 11; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| DEX-10 | Each outbound file is sent with a Records Transmittal (GS-F-44). The transmittal and the Registrar's acknowledgement, when one is given, are filed (OQ-32). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |

#### 4.24.6 Exceptions and unusual cases

- A file is incomplete or a column is blank: the row is held and GS staff ask for a corrected file (Consultation 20 July 2026 discussion; handled as in 4.1).
- The Registrar does not acknowledge a file: the case stays at Sent to Registrar; GS staff follow up and the platform does not record receipt unless GS staff enter it (OQ-32).
- AIMS shows a student as enrolled while the Graduate School record says otherwise: the AIMS value is followed and the difference is flagged (DEX-05).
- No export from AIMS is supplied in a term: the Academic Coordinator's monitoring workbook and class lists remain the working record (OQ-33).

#### 4.24.7 Records produced and where they go

**Forms of this process: GS-P-06 AIMS and Registrar Data Exchange Specification. Appendix A gives the name each has in the Handbook or the Protocol.**

| Record | Where it goes |
|---|---|
| GS-P-06 AIMS and Registrar Data Exchange Specification | Graduate School; AIMS and Registrar owner |
| Uploaded workbooks and class lists with the import result | Platform; GS staff |
| Daily change report with the reflected mark | Platform; Academic Coordinator and GS staff |
| Leave and readmission export log; endorsed graduation list | Platform; GS staff; Dean |
| GS-F-44 Records Transmittal and Registrar acknowledgement | GS staff; Registrar |

#### 4.24.8 How the platform supports this process

GS staff upload the Academic Coordinator's monitoring workbook at handoff; GS staff and the Academic Coordinator upload class lists. The platform shows a daily change report of the changes the Registrar must apply in AIMS, with a reflected mark and the time and person. It exports Dean-approved leave and readmission cases as CSV or Excel with an export log and status changes, and the Dean exports the endorsed graduation list as CSV. Imported official data are read-only, and the proposed AIMS export workbook is kept in Documents/Sample_Data/AIMS_Export/.

**Difference between the platform and the rule.** The platform does not read the proposed AIMS export workbook (PROPOSED-1.0) yet; the working inputs are the Academic Coordinator's monitoring workbook and the class lists. The Registrar's acknowledgement is recorded by GS staff for a graduation list (Sent to Registrar, then Registrar Received), a withdrawal list and a leave export. The cadence in DEX-04 and the exact columns are proposals (OQ-50).

A machine-readable copy is saved as operations_manual_rules.json. The status column is Official (Handbook or Protocol), Practice (described by staff in consultation) or Proposed (the group's proposal); Practice and Proposed rows must be confirmed by the Graduate School.

| WD-08 | Withdrawal of subject | The accomplished Withdrawal Form is provided to the Registrar, the Accounting Office, and the student. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49 | Official |
| WD-09 | Withdrawal of subject | The grade symbol W means Withdrawn. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| WD-10 | Withdrawal of subject | A subject withdrawal (from one subject, not from the Graduate School) before or in the first week of classes does not count against the student; dropping is the act of leaving a subject in the middle of the semester. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| WD-11 | Withdrawal of subject | An approved withdrawal is sent to the Registrar, which processes it in the registration system, and then to the Business Office for any fee arrangement. The Graduate School's responsibility ends at sending the request to the Registrar. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| WD-12 | Withdrawal of subject | After the Registrar has been sent the request, the Graduate School tags the subject as withdrawn (a manual tag is enough), with the date applied, the date granted and a text area for the result. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| WD-13 | Withdrawal of subject | According to the Associate Dean, a student may withdraw before classes begin or in the first week of classes; the Dean decides with discretion, and a request submitted after the deadline or with an insufficient or inconsistent justification is normally denied. (This is a second-hand account recorded by the group and conflicts with the Handbook's second week; see OQ-01.) | Consultation 29 July 2026, account of the Associate Dean's answer | Practice |
| WD-14 | Withdrawal of subject | The platform shows the student and the staff the Handbook's fee bracket for the date of the request (10% in the first week, 20% in the second week, full fees after the window) as information only; the Business Office computes the fee. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 49, 51 | Proposed |
| DRP-01 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | Students are expected to attend classes regularly; the maximum number of absences allowed a student is 20% of the total number of class hours for the whole semester. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| DRP-02 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A student may be excused for a death in the family, illness (with a medical certificate from the USLS clinic or a personal doctor), or a graduate program activity endorsed by the Dean. Excuses are for time missed only, and all work missed must be made up to the satisfaction of the professor within a reasonable time. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| DRP-03 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | When unexcused absence reaches more than 20% of the stipulated hours in one course, the student is automatically dropped from the course by the professor and is given a grade of DRP with a 5.0 grade. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| DRP-04 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A student who takes part in a school-sponsored activity or represents the University in an official capacity is granted an Approved Absence on the Dean's approval; it is not counted against the allowed absences. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| DRP-05 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | The grade symbols D (Dropped) and W (Withdrawn) appear in the grading system; students with grades INC, D or W, or who repeated a subject, are not eligible for the special academic honors. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 53, 55 | Official |
| DRP-06 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | If a student gets a 5.0 he or she is automatically dropped from the program; failure in any subject means no re-admission to the program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| DRP-07 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A student drops a subject during the semester by talking or writing to the Academic Coordinator. Once agreed, the Academic Coordinator or GS staff tag the subject as dropped with a date and notes. No student-facing drop request is needed in the Graduate School's record. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| DRP-08 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | The Graduate School drops the subject in AIMS as well as in its own record. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| DRP-09 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A drop agreed between the student and the Academic Coordinator is recorded by the Academic Coordinator without a separate Graduate School approval step (there is no Deny); the record stays marked as awaiting confirmation until the Graduate School has seen the change in AIMS. (The platform has no student drop request; see DRP-03 for the absence limit it enforces.) | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, items 18, 19 and 79 (revision adopted after the 15 July adviser session) | Proposed |
| DRP-10 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A student's request to leave a single subject up to the end of the second week of classes is handled as a withdrawal (see WD-01); after that the Handbook gives no way to withdraw a single subject, and a subject is dropped by the professor for unexcused absences above 20% (DRP-03). The dividing line is the group's reading of the Associate Dean's distinction between withdrawal and dropping; what a student who wants to leave one subject after the second week may do is OQ-03. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49; Consultation 21 July 2026 | Proposed |
| DRP-11 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | To remain in good standing a master's student must maintain a weighted average of 2.0 or better, and a doctorate student 1.75 or better, at the end of each academic year; a grade of 3.0 (master's) or 2.0 (doctorate) is passing but carries no graduate credit. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| DRP-12 | Dropping, including being dropped for absences | A grade of INC may be given if the class standing is passing but the student fails to take the final examination or complete other requirements for illness or other valid reasons. It is removed within one academic year by meeting all course requirements and submitting the Incomplete Grade Removal Form to the Graduate School Office within the set deadline; after a year without compliance the grade becomes 3.0 (master's) or 2.0 (doctorate) and the subject is taken again; a student with INC in all subjects is dropped from the rolls of the Graduate School. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| LOA-01 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A student who does not intend to enroll in a semester may apply for a leave of absence; a student on leave does not sever his or her ties with the University. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| LOA-02 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A request for a leave of absence is made in writing to the Dean and states the reason for the leave and specifies the period. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| LOA-03 | Leave of absence (LOA) | The leave may be approved for a period of one (1) year but may be renewed for at most another year. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| LOA-04 | Leave of absence (LOA) | LOA must be done on or before the deadline. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52 | Official |
| LOA-05 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A student who does not intend to enroll for a valid reason (medical reasons) but still has remaining units to enroll is not allowed to enroll in Residency but should file for a Leave of Absence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| LOA-06 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A student may file an LOA if the student withdraws after two weeks of classes have elapsed; the student is subject to a withdrawal fee from the Cashier's office. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-07 | Leave of absence (LOA) | The Dean, Associate Dean, or the duly authorized representative informs the University Registrar of the leave, indicating the reasons and the amount of money refunded to the student, if any. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-08 | Leave of absence (LOA) | For a leave availed of by the student during the second half of the semester, the class standing of the courses enrolled is W (withdrawn); no refund of tuition and fees is provided. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-09 | Leave of absence (LOA) | No leave of absence is granted during the semester within two (2) weeks before the last day of classes. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-10 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A master's program must be finished within seven academic years and a doctoral program within nine academic years, including the leave of absence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| LOA-11 | Leave of absence (LOA) | A student who withdraws from the college without a formal leave of absence is considered absent without leave (AWOL) and has registration privileges curtailed or entirely withdrawn. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| LOA-12 | Leave of absence (LOA) | The student may file the leave through a structured form in place of a letter, provided the Dean accepts the form as the written request. | Group working notes, Consultation 20 July 2026 rehearsal (structured form suggested by the adviser) | Proposed |
| LOA-13 | Leave of absence (LOA) | The platform checks a leave request for an allowed reason category, a written reason, a valid start and end semester (at most two semesters per request and four in total with earlier approved leave), the filing date (not within fourteen days before the last day of classes) and the prototype minimum of one completed semester, and gives the Dean a recommendation; the Dean decides. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): LOA policy review | Proposed |
| RDM-01 | Return from leave of absence and readmission | A student returning from a leave of absence (LOA) or absence without leave (AWOL) declares his or her intention to enroll by writing to the University Registrar through the Graduate School Dean, who then endorses it to the University Registrar. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| RDM-02 | Return from leave of absence and readmission | A returnee must present: (1) clearance from the Business Office, (2) an interview (reason for delay, residency requirements), (3) evaluation of subject and grade requirements, (4) an Advise Slip from the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 45 | Official |
| RDM-03 | Return from leave of absence and readmission | Failure in any subject means no re-admission to the program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RDM-04 | Return from leave of absence and readmission | A student who cannot finish within the maximum residence (seven years for a master's, nine for a doctorate) must re-enroll all courses taken and earn new credits. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RDM-05 | Return from leave of absence and readmission | The platform's readmission checklist asks for a structured return intention, a confirmed updated study plan, a program or adviser consultation, and confirmation that no accountability is pending. The Handbook's four requirements (RDM-02) are the official list. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): readmission requirements | Proposed |
| AWL-01 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | A student who withdraws from a college without a formal leave of absence is considered in "absent without leave" status (AWOL) and has registration privileges curtailed or entirely withdrawn. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| AWL-02 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | A student returning from AWOL declares the intention to enroll by writing to the University Registrar through the Graduate School Dean, who endorses it to the Registrar. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| AWL-03 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | A returnee presents a Business Office clearance, an interview, an evaluation of subject and grade requirements and an Advise Slip from the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 45 | Official |
| AWL-04 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | An AWOL status has the same effect as a leave of absence for reporting: it is communicated to the Registrar. | Consultation 20 July 2026, adviser's statement; not confirmed by Graduate School staff | Proposed |
| AWL-05 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | AWOL is flagged from source evidence (an imported AWOL standing, or a semester enrollment marked Withdrawn with no approved leave and no resolved return), not typed in by a user and not inferred from absence alone. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): AWOL policy review | Proposed |
| AWL-06 | Absence without leave (AWOL) | On return, the student is classified by years in the program: within the normal limit (five years master's, seven years doctorate), extension with a graded six-unit refresher (up to seven and nine), or full re-enrollment beyond that. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 54-55 | Proposed |
| RES-01 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Students who have finished all their coursework and are just working on their thesis or dissertation and practicum or internship, after a semester of enrollment for thesis writing and practicum, may enroll for residence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-02 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Students who are not enrolled in any subject but who want to complete an INC, will take the comprehensive exam, or are waiting for their research publication must also enroll for residence. Registration for residence should be done within the semester. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-03 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | A student who does not intend to enroll for a valid reason but still has remaining units is not allowed to enroll in Residency, but files a leave of absence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| RES-04 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | For the comprehensive examination the Academic Coordinator does the initial evaluation and the student is advised to enroll in Residency in Comprehensive Examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| RES-05 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Master's program: candidacy must be attained within three academic years from the date of admission and all requirements completed within five academic years. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RES-06 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Master's program: a student who cannot comply within five years is allowed a maximum two-year extension but must enroll in a graded six-unit refresher course related to the area of specialization. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54 | Official |
| RES-07 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | A master's program must be finished within a maximum of seven academic years including leave of absence; a student who cannot finish within seven years must re-enroll all the courses taken and earn new credits. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-08 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Doctorate program: candidacy within five academic years from admission; all requirements including the dissertation within seven academic years. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-09 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Doctorate program: beyond seven years a maximum two-year extension with a graded six-unit refresher course; the program must be finished within nine academic years including leave of absence, after which the student must re-enroll all courses taken and earn credit units. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55 | Official |
| RES-10 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | Thesis or project paper writing must be finished within two years from the title or proposal defense; dissertation writing within three years from the title defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| RES-11 | Residency enrollment and maximum residence | The platform records each residency enrollment with its purpose (thesis or dissertation work, practicum or internship, comprehensive examination, awaiting publication) and marks a purpose as unsupported if the student's record does not match it; staff may still record it with notes that explain the exception. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): residency policy review | Proposed |
| CEX-01 | Comprehensive examination | The comprehensive examinations cover all basic or core courses, fields of concentration, and electives or cognates. They are given for two days for a master's and three days for a doctorate, with two-day intervals between examinations or consecutively as arranged, and last a maximum of eight hours per day. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-02 | Comprehensive examination | Master's scope: first day basic courses and electives or cognates; second day major courses. Doctorate scope: first day basic courses and electives or cognates; second and third days major courses. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-03 | Comprehensive examination | Master's students who have passed all academic requirements and been evaluated by their Graduate School Academic Coordinators may file an application; doctorate students evaluated by their College Deans may likewise file. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-04 | Comprehensive examination | Non-thesis students must have completed the integrating courses (feasibility study, project study, case study or applied research in the major field for MBA; action research or practicum in the major field for Education). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| CEX-05 | Comprehensive examination | The application is filed with the Graduate Program Secretary at least two weeks before the scheduled examination; an examinee who withdraws the application informs the Graduate Program in writing with the reason at least three working days before the examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 56-57 | Official |
| CEX-06 | Comprehensive examination | The examination fee is P1,100 for a master's and P2,500 for a doctorate, paid to the Business Office; the official receipt attached to the application is presented to the secretary before the exam. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-07 | Comprehensive examination | The fee is forfeited if the examinee fails to appear during the examination dates or fails to inform the Graduate Program Services Office by a written note. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-08 | Comprehensive examination | A student must get a minimum score of 7.00 points (70%) in all courses to pass. A student below 7.00 may take a first retake in the failed courses within the residency enrollment and is allowed two retakes within the semester. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-09 | Comprehensive examination | A student who fails the second retake takes a refresher course on the failed courses the following semester and may take the examination for the third and last time in the following residency enrollment; failure the fourth time means disqualification from the program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-10 | Comprehensive examination | A student who passes the comprehensive examination confers with the Dean for a thesis or dissertation adviser and is considered advanced to candidacy. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57 | Official |
| CEX-11 | Comprehensive examination | Passing the comprehensive examination is a graduation requirement; for special academic honors the examination must be passed with no retake. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 55, 77 | Official |
| CEX-12 | Comprehensive examination | The monitoring workbook records the comprehensive examination in its own column. | Group working notes, Monitoring workbook, COMPRE column | Practice |
| CEX-13 | Comprehensive examination | The platform keeps all research stages from the title defense onward locked until the comprehensive examination status is Passed and all curriculum subjects are complete; the Graduate School can switch the examination requirement off in the register. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): comprehensive exam eligibility | Proposed |
| TTL-01 | Title defense | The application for title defense (Form 1 and three concept papers to the Academic Coordinator) must be made at least two weeks before the scheduled defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 2 note | Official |
| TTL-02 | Title defense | The Research Coordinator nominates the panel members as recommended by the Academic Coordinator, after deliberation with the GS Research Committee. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-03 | Title defense | Panel for a project paper: one panel chair and two members (one content specialist and one method specialist). Thesis or bridging thesis: one chair and three members (one content specialist, one method specialist and one external panel). Dissertation: one chair and four members (two content specialists, one method specialist and one external panel). The chair and the content and method specialists are University-affiliated; the external panel is a content or industry expert who takes part from the proposal defense onwards. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-04 | Title defense | The panel members deliberate the three submitted titles without the presence of the student; the chair then admits the student. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part II | Official |
| TTL-05 | Title defense | The panel chair may record the defense with the consent of everyone in the meeting room and furnish the student the link of the recording. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60 | Official |
| TTL-06 | Title defense | The panel chair emails the accomplished Form 2 to the Research Coordinator and the student within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 2 | Official |
| TTL-07 | Title defense | If the proposed titles are disapproved, the student presents a new set of three titles and follows the same protocol; the decision is reflected in Form 2. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 3 | Official |
| TTL-08 | Title defense | The student asks the panel about their preferred mode of payment, settles the title defense fees and emails the proof to the GS mailbox within the first working day after the defense, with the subject line LAST NAME_type of defense_receipt. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part III, step 4 | Official |
| TTL-09 | Title defense | Thesis or project paper writing must be finished within two years from the title or proposal defense; for the thesis the student has one year from the title defense to submit and defend a proposal. Dissertation writing must be finished within three years from the title defense, with one year to submit and defend a proposal. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| TTL-10 | Title defense | If the approved title is still applicable, the same concept paper may be presented for re-defense; otherwise the student changes the research title or topic and goes through the title defense again. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| TTL-11 | Title defense | GS staff check that Form 1, three concept papers and the Academic Coordinator's endorsement are present before the Research Coordinator nominates a panel. The check is for completeness only; in the platform the endorsement is refused unless Form 1 and all three papers are uploaded, and panel matching opens only after it. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 42; Group working notes, transactions list | Proposed |
| TTL-12 | Title defense | The platform does not schedule a title defense, nominate a panel or approve a title by itself; panel candidates are suggested from the uploaded concept papers, faculty specialization, availability and workload, and staff may choose any active faculty member. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 44 | Proposed |
| ADV-01 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The research adviser must be affiliated with the University and the degree program must be aligned with the student's program. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, step 1 | Official |
| ADV-02 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Protocol: the research adviser must have a PhD degree for PhD students and at least PhD units for MA students (CHED CMO 15), and should have a maximum of five active advisees for the current academic year. | Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, step 1 note | Official |
| ADV-03 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Handbook: the adviser shall be a faculty of the Graduate School and a holder of a doctoral degree aligned with the discipline, with at least one publication in a refereed journal or publicly recognized creative or technology outputs (CMO 15 s. 2019). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-04 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | In the exigency of service a faculty member may have more than five advisees with the approval of the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-05 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The student may express a preference, but the Dean, the Associate Dean and the Research Coordinator make the final decision based on the degree, expertise, research work, number of advisees (maximum five per semester) and total workload of the faculty member. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-06 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The choice of adviser gives primary importance to the student's choice with the approval of the program adviser and the Dean; the Dean appoints the adviser on the basis of expertise in the student's topic following the Research Protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-07 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Faculty of other universities may serve as adviser in some circumstances if they are adjunct faculty of the Graduate School. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-08 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The assignment of an adviser may be done while the student is still taking academic courses. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-09 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Form 3 is emailed by the student to the Academic Coordinator, who notes it to the Research Coordinator; the Dean's e-signature is not required at that point; the Research Coordinator emails Form 3 to the Dean; the Research Coordinator, the Associate Dean and the Dean sign; the Research Coordinator emails Form 3.1 to the student and adviser, who email the accomplished form back. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 61; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part I, steps 2-5 | Official |
| ADV-10 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The advisee and adviser accomplish Form 3.2 and Form 3.3; the student emails them to the Research Coordinator within five days of receipt of Form 3.1. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 61-62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser, Part II | Official |
| ADV-11 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The adviser is bound only to advising and shall not be selected as editor of the student or to any other appointment related to the conduct of the study. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-12 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The adviser monitors the research according to the timetable in Form 3.2, reviews submitted proposals, questionnaires and chapters, gives technical and moral guidance, conducts a mock defense when necessary, attends the defense of the advisee, attends orientations set by the Graduate School, and adheres to the Research Protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59 | Official |
| ADV-13 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | A student may make only one request to change the title of the study, before the proposal defense application: Form 3.4 and the new concept paper go to the adviser, who endorses the request to the panel; the panel deliberates and the chair emails the decision. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Change of Title of the Study | Official |
| ADV-14 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | A student may make only one request to change the research adviser, before the proposal defense, for justifiable reasons: Form 3.1.1 signed by the current and new adviser, deliberated by the Research Coordinator, Associate Dean and Dean; then a new Form 3.1. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Request for Change of Research Adviser | Official |
| ADV-15 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | An adviser designated before the effectivity of his or her retirement may continue to serve until the final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58 | Official |
| ADV-16 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | The student needs to propose three faculty as adviser options. | Consultation 21 July 2026, Associate Dean's description | Practice |
| ADV-17 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Each nominated faculty member may accept or decline the nomination (the Associate Dean suggested that the platform should offer this; it does not yet); the student and the Graduate School then choose among those who accept. | Consultation 21 July 2026, Associate Dean's suggestion | Proposed |
| ADV-18 | Adviser designation, advising contract, change of title and change of adviser | Adviser-dependent steps (adviser signature, endorsement, document review, availability in scheduling) cannot be completed until an adviser is designated. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 43 | Proposed |
| PRP-01 | Proposal defense and ethics review | Before the proposal defense the student must have consulted a statistician for a quantitative study, with Form 4.1 accomplished by the statistician. Form 4.1 is not required for qualitative studies. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (statistical consultation) | Official |
| PRP-02 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The research adviser accomplishes Form 4 with e-signature once the paper complies with the research paper guidelines set by the Graduate School Office; this must be done at least two weeks before the date of the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (Form 4 endorsement and note) | Official |
| PRP-03 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The panel members are given two weeks to review the paper before the scheduled proposal defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (panel review period) | Official |
| PRP-04 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The adviser's recommendation of an external panel is subject to the approval of the GS Research Committee (Dean, Associate Dean, Academic Coordinator, Research Coordinator) according to the Protocol, and to the approval of the GS office according to the Handbook. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 63; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (external panel recommendation) | Official |
| PRP-05 | Proposal defense and ethics review | For co-authorship, the student must have the major part and contribute at least 80% during the conduct of the study and must adhere to the contribution of work in Form 4.2; co-authorship or study grant agreements follow the institutional guidelines of the Center for Research and Engagement. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part II (co-authorship note) | Official |
| PRP-06 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The panel deliberates the paper first without the student and the adviser; if major revisions are recommended the student is advised to do a re-defense following the same protocol, reflected in Form 5. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part II | Official |
| PRP-07 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The panel chair emails the consolidated Form 5 to the student, the adviser and the Research Coordinator within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 64; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (consolidated Form 5) | Official |
| PRP-08 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The application for ethics review (Form 5.2 with the revised manuscript and Form 5.1) is made within one month after the proposal defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (ethics review note) | Official |
| PRP-09 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The student settles payment to the preferred method of the panel members and emails the proof to the GS mailbox within the first working day after the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part III (payment) | Official |
| PRP-10 | Proposal defense and ethics review | A student who has used a change of title or a change of adviser must have done so before the proposal defense application (ADV-13, ADV-14). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 62 | Official |
| PRP-11 | Proposal defense and ethics review | Ethics clearance is needed before the closed-door final defense: the adviser emails Form 4, the Ethics Clearance and the manuscript to the Research Coordinator. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (Form 4 submission) | Official |
| PRP-12 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The Research Coordinator records ethics clearance as received; the office checks that the document is present, not its content. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 42 | Proposed |
| PRP-13 | Proposal defense and ethics review | The platform does not complete the proposal stage without the adviser's signature on the manuscript and on Form 4, and it asks every student for Form 4.1 (it does not distinguish quantitative from qualitative studies). | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 43; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| FIN-01 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Final decisions are pass, pass with minor revisions, and provisional pass with major revisions (subject for re-defense). | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-02 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | The adviser emails Form 4, the Ethics Clearance and the manuscript to the Research Coordinator at least two weeks before the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (Form 4 submission) | Official |
| FIN-03 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | 14-Day Rule: "At least fourteen (14) days, inclusive of weekends, from the date the panel members of the oral defense received their copies of the thesis/dissertation to the date of the oral defense." | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I note | Official |
| FIN-04 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | The same set of panel members takes part in the closed-door final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I (same panel) | Official |
| FIN-05 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | The panel members must not agree with the proposed schedule without the revised manuscript; the manuscript must already have been given to the panel before the date is set. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I note | Official |
| FIN-06 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | If a provisional pass with major revisions is given and the panel recommends a re-defense, the student follows the same protocol for the final defense afterward; the same amount of fees applies to the re-defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II note | Official |
| FIN-07 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | If a provisional pass with major revisions is given but no re-defense is recommended, the student complies with the corrections; once the revised manuscript is given to the panel for approval the 14-Day Rule applies; the adviser accomplishes Form 8 and emails it to the GS Research Coordinator. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-08 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | A student who fails to meet the recommendations of all the panel members at a given time is advised to re-defend following the same protocol. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-09 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | A provisional pass with major revisions must be reflected in Form 6 and agree with the score in Form 7: for a thesis the score is below 85; for a dissertation the score is below 90. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part II note | Official |
| FIN-10 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Each panel member accomplishes Form 6 and emails it to the panel chair, and accomplishes Form 7 and emails it to the Research Coordinator, within the first working day after the defense (the Protocol adds that the research adviser also accomplishes Form 7); the chair emails all Forms 6 and the consolidated comments to the Research Coordinator, adviser and student within the same time. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| FIN-11 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | For students admitted on August 2020 and onwards, the student is given two to four weeks to prepare and apply for the public final defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 71; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part IV | Official |
| FIN-12 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Form 4.3 must be fully accomplished with the e-signature of the panel members and the date the panel received the revised manuscript. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 72-73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (Form 4.3 notes) | Official |
| FIN-13 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Form 6 must state the Action Taken by the student to address each comment and the specific page number where the improvement appears. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 72 | Official |
| FIN-14 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Application for the public final defense must be made 5 days before the preferred schedule for posting purposes; Form 4.3 must be submitted at least two weeks before the date of the defense. (The two periods conflict; see OQ-23.) | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 72-73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (Form 4.3 notes) | Official |
| FIN-15 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | The announcement of the public defense is posted on the Graduate School Official Facebook Page with an embedded pre-registration link; the Research Coordinator coordinates campus entry with the GS Office. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (announcement and pre-registration) | Official |
| FIN-16 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Students enrolled (admitted) before August 2020 are not required to do a public final defense; instead, after the closed-door final defense, the student presents at a research conference. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 68-69; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III note | Official |
| FIN-17 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | In the public final defense the student has 30 minutes to present; the Handbook adds another 30 minutes for questions and answers, and the audience may ask questions. Panel members must have Form 6 in hand to check compliance. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part II | Official |
| FIN-18 | Final defense (closed-door and public) | Only the panel chair submits the defense verdict and the record of the result. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 48 | Proposed |
| CMP-01 | Revisions and completion | After the student revises the paper, the manuscript is emailed to the panel members for their approval before Turnitin scanning and editing; the 14-Day Rule is applied, and the panel members then email the student confirming their initial approval. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III-IV | Official |
| CMP-02 | Revisions and completion | The adviser verifies that the panelists' recommendations are reflected in the manuscript and then endorses it for Turnitin scanning and editing. | Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III (adviser verification) | Official |
| CMP-03 | Revisions and completion | The final draft undergoes Turnitin scanning; a similarity index rating (SIR) of not more than 15% is required before the paper goes to the editor. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| CMP-04 | Revisions and completion | The student chooses from the pool of editors affiliated with the University so that the USLS IRF is employed; the 14-Day Rule applies to the editor; the editor gives Form 9, Editor Certification, once corrections are carried out. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| CMP-05 | Revisions and completion | The student sends the soft copy of the final manuscript to the GS Office, copy furnished to the research ethics office, with the panel approval emails, ethics clearance, Turnitin certificate and editor's certification, within the period set by the office, to inform the office that the manuscript is ready for hard-bound reproduction. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68 | Official |
| CMP-06 | Revisions and completion | The GS Office coordinates with the panel members and advisers for the accomplishment of Form 10, Approval Sheet. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| CMP-07 | Revisions and completion | The student submits the bound copies and CD with the signed approval sheets as part of the requirements for the diploma, TOR and other related documents. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 68; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part III | Official |
| CMP-08 | Revisions and completion | The student presents the thesis or dissertation at a research conference (the Graduate School Research Conference or an external one); the certificate of presentation for an external conference may be submitted after the bound copies and CD. Only graduating students present at the Graduate School Research Conference and join the graduation ceremony. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 68-69, 74; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part III note | Official |
| CMP-09 | Revisions and completion | Publication: the student chooses a journal from the list of the Publication and Engagement Office and accomplishes Form 12 (Scopus, ISI and Web of Science for PhD; peer-reviewed journals for MA students); the manuscript, ethics clearance (if applicable), Form 11 (if applicable) and Form 12 go to the GS Research Coordinator for review by the Research Committee (Research Coordinator, Academic Coordinator, Associate Dean and the external expert); the Dean approves; the student then files Form 13 with the copy of the journal or the certificate of publication. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 75 | Official |
| CMP-10 | Revisions and completion | The student can only be declared a graduate and join the graduation ceremony once Form 13 and a copy of the journal or certificate of publication has been submitted. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 75 | Official |
| CMP-11 | Revisions and completion | The published journal must include the authorship affiliation in the sample format given in the Handbook. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 75 | Official |
| CMP-12 | Revisions and completion | The best thesis or dissertation award requires a cut-off score of 96 points; the chairman of the committee or the Dean submits the work to the committee and approval by the majority is required. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 76-77 | Official |
| CMP-13 | Revisions and completion | The office checks that each completion document is present and that the Turnitin certificate shows a rating of 15% or below; it does not verify the content of the documents. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 42; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| SCH-01 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | For the title defense the GS Office coordinates with the panel members about the proposed schedule; the Research Coordinator informs the panel and the student of the date and time. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 60; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Title Defense, Part I, steps 5-6 | Official |
| SCH-02 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | For the proposal and final defenses the student and the research adviser agree with the panel members on the date and time, and the agreed schedule is indicated in Form 4. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 63, 65; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (agreed schedule) | Official |
| SCH-03 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | Lead times: title defense application at least two weeks before the defense; Form 4 at least two weeks before the proposal or final defense; the panel has two weeks to review the manuscript; under the 14-Day Rule at least fourteen days, inclusive of weekends, pass from the date the panel receives the manuscript to the defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 59, 63, 65-66; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Closed-door Final Defense, Part I note | Official |
| SCH-04 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | Public final defense: Form 4.3 must be submitted five days before the preferred schedule for posting purposes (and at least two weeks before the defense according to the same section; OQ-23). | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 72-73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Public Defense, Part I (Form 4.3 notes) | Official |
| SCH-05 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | The panel chair coordinates with the GS Office for the venue (proposal and closed-door final defense); the GS Office reserves the venue for the public final defense. For students abroad the defense is online. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 63, 66, 70, 73; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part I (venue and students abroad) | Official |
| SCH-06 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | The panel members must not agree with a proposed schedule without the revised manuscript. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 65 | Official |
| SCH-07 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | A re-defense follows the same protocol as the original defense. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 64, 66-67; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Proposal Defense, Part II (major revisions) | Official |
| SCH-08 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | Faculty invited to a panel may accept the invitation for the proposed date or mark themselves unavailable (the Associate Dean suggested this). | Consultation 21 July 2026, Associate Dean's suggestion for panel scheduling | Proposed |
| SCH-09 | Defense scheduling and rescheduling | A defense is confirmed only when all required panel members and the adviser are available on the date; the 14-day lead time is a hard rule that staff cannot override; the student may send a scheduling request with preferred dates; a reschedule or cancellation needs a recorded reason and the name of who asked for it. | Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, section 9; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| PRC-01 | Practicum | Students who have finished all their coursework and are just working on the practicum or internship, after a semester of enrollment for it, may enroll for residence. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 48 | Official |
| PRC-02 | Practicum | For the non-thesis track in Education, the integrating course (action research or practicum in the major field) must be completed before the student files for the comprehensive examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 56 | Official |
| PRC-03 | Practicum | The curriculum copied in the group's notes for the Doctor of Philosophy in Psychology lists DPSY330 Internship (six units) in the second semester of the second year, before the written comprehensive examination and the dissertation. (The document the curriculum was copied from is not identified in the notes.) | Group working notes, 'Course stuff' section, program curriculum tables | Practice |
| PRC-04 | Practicum | The practicum workflow applies only to students in Psychology programs and in the Master of Science in Guidance and Counseling (MSGC); students of other programs are excluded. | Group working notes, Proposal and scenario notes, practicum program restriction; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| PRC-05 | Practicum | A student may begin the practicum only after the platform's check of curriculum completion and research progress is satisfied; if source data are missing the check shows Needs verification, not Pass. | Group working notes, Platform (current build): practicum eligibility | Proposed |
| PRC-06 | Practicum | The student submits a signed MOA, the practicum site and the supervisor's name; GS staff record and forward it; the Academic Coordinator reviews it and marks the practicum as in progress. | Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, section 11 | Proposed |
| PRC-07 | Practicum | The required practicum hours default to 200; the practicum cannot be marked completed below the required hours; completion evidence is one or more certificate PDFs, and several certificates are allowed when the hours come from different companies. | Group working notes, Platform (current build); proposal notes | Proposed |
| PRC-08 | Practicum | If the hours are short, the Academic Coordinator returns the case with a reason; the student submits additional evidence; the earlier files stay in the history and are not overwritten. | Group working notes, Scenario notes, practicum unusual case | Proposed |
| PRC-09 | Practicum | The Academic Coordinator verifies hours and documents and sends a completed status report to the Dean; the Dean reviews the report and marks it reviewed. The Dean does not approve or deny the practicum. | Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, section 11 | Proposed |
| GRD-01 | Graduation application and endorsement | A student will only be considered a graduate of the University after completing all the requirements listed below; a student will not be allowed to go up the stage if requirements are not met. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-02 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must be officially enrolled during the semester of application for graduation. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-03 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have completed all the prescribed subjects for the degree. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-04 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have passed the comprehensive examination. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-05 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have completed a successful final oral defense of the project paper, thesis or dissertation, and complied with the required revisions. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-06 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must submit one bound approved copy of the project paper, thesis or dissertation and one CD. The bound copy conforms to the standard format of the University and contains the official approval by the members of the defense panel and the official acceptance by the Dean. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-07 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have paid all financial obligations to the University; the Graduate School may withhold reports of grades, transcripts of records and diplomas of students who have not fully paid. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77; Handbook 2022-2023, p. 51 | Official |
| GRD-08 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have presented the thesis or dissertation in a public seminar series. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77 | Official |
| GRD-09 | Graduation application and endorsement | The student must have published at least one research paper while enrolled in the program in a Scopus-indexed, Web of Science and ISI journal, for those enrolled from AY 2022-2023 onwards. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 77; Handbook 2022-2023, p. 75 | Official |
| GRD-10 | Graduation application and endorsement | Graduating students are required to attend the Alumni seminar given by the Alumni Director. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 80 | Official |
| GRD-11 | Graduation application and endorsement | The Office of the Registrar certifies the eligibility of candidates for graduation and honors. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 32 | Official |
| GRD-12 | Graduation application and endorsement | Academic honors: Highest Academic Honor needs a GPA of 1.1 or better and no grade lower than 1.25; High Academic Honor needs a GPA of 1.25 or better and no grade lower than 1.25 (doctoral) or 1.5 (master's). Both need moral and academic integrity, no repeated subject, no INC, D or W grade, and passing the comprehensive examination with no re-take. | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 55-56 | Official |
| GRD-13 | Graduation application and endorsement | GS staff compile the candidate list; the Academic Coordinator checks coursework; the Research Coordinator validates the research completion documents; GS staff prepare the endorsement list; the Dean approves it and sends it to the Registrar. | Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, section 12 | Proposed |
| GRD-14 | Graduation application and endorsement | A student is eligible for selection into a graduation batch only after coursework, research and practicum (where applicable) are complete and the student has submitted a signed graduation application; an application with incorrect information is returned and replaced by a completely new corrected document, the original being kept. | Group working notes, Scenario notes, graduation standard and unusual cases; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| GRD-15 | Graduation application and endorsement | Candidates are reviewed within a graduation review window for a school year opened and closed by the Graduate School; the dates, the treatment of late applicants and the treatment of eligible students who miss the window are to be set by the Graduate School. | Group working notes, Scenario notes; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| GRD-16 | Graduation application and endorsement | After the Dean approves a batch, the endorsed list is exported as a file and the Dean sends it to the Registrar by the official email; the platform records the export and the status Exported — Ready to Send, and does not record that an email was sent or that the Registrar acknowledged it. | Group working notes, Scenario notes; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| GRD-17 | Graduation application and endorsement | A project paper, thesis or dissertation is nominated for the best thesis or dissertation award by the chairman of the committee or by the Dean; it is scored out of 100 points (format and content 15, analysis and discussion 40, findings, action plan or program 15, originality, novelty and contribution to new knowledge or applicability 30) and needs a cut-off of 96 points and the approval of the majority of the committee (see also CMP-12). | Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 76-77 | Official |
| HND-01 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The accomplished Withdrawal Form is provided to the Registrar, the Accounting Office, and the student. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49 | Official |
| HND-02 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The Dean, Associate Dean or authorized representative informs the University Registrar of a leave of absence, indicating the reasons and the amount of money refunded to the student, if any. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| HND-03 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The Graduate School Dean endorses a returning student's written intention to enroll to the University Registrar. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53 | Official |
| HND-04 | Records handoff to the Registrar | Cross-enrollment: the Dean's written permission is presented to the Registrar, who issues the cross-enrollment permit; after the course the student submits the grade to the Registrar for inclusion in the academic record. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 50 | Official |
| HND-05 | Records handoff to the Registrar | A professor submits his or her grades within one year after the course was enrolled; an error in grades is corrected after the professor explains in writing to the Dean and secures approval, before the Registrar submits grades to CHED. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49 | Official |
| HND-06 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The Registrar is the repository of the records of academic performance and certifies the eligibility of candidates for graduation and honors. | Handbook 2022-2023, p. 32 | Official |
| HND-07 | Records handoff to the Registrar | For a withdrawal, the request is sent to the Registrar, who processes it in the system, and the Business Office then arranges any tuition adjustment; the Graduate School's responsibility ends at sending the request to the Registrar. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| HND-08 | Records handoff to the Registrar | There is no integration between AIMS and the Graduate School record. The Graduate School cannot pull data from AIMS and may ask the ITS for an Excel export. | Consultation 21 July 2026 | Practice |
| HND-09 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The Graduate School keeps a log of each handoff (what, when, by whom, to which office) and of the Registrar's confirmation when one is received; the platform does not claim that the Registrar has acted until a confirmation is recorded. | Group working notes, July 20 checklist, item 57; Consultation 20 July 2026, adviser's remark that the Registrar must confirm | Proposed |
| HND-10 | Records handoff to the Registrar | The export for the Registrar is a file (for withdrawals, an Excel list of approved withdrawals; for graduation, a CSV list of endorsed candidates) sent by the responsible person by email; column layouts are to be confirmed by the Registrar. | Group working notes, Scenario notes; Platform (current build) | Proposed |
| FAC-01 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | Each faculty member has one profile with name, college or department, specialization, email, eligible roles (Faculty Adviser, Panel Member, Panel Chair, Academic Coordinator, Research Coordinator) and an Active or Inactive status. GS staff and the Academic Coordinator create and edit profiles. | Platform (current build), faculty profile | Proposed |
| FAC-02 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | A faculty member lists preferred subjects in order of preference on GS-F-47. A faculty member may change only the member's own list; GS staff and the Academic Coordinator may enter it on the member's behalf. Only subjects of the member's own college may be listed. | Platform (current build), faculty preferred subjects | Proposed |
| FAC-03 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | A preferred subject is a wish, not a promise. The Academic Coordinator weighs preference with availability, eligible role, qualification and current load, and the Academic Coordinator's assignment stands (see CRS-07 and CRS-12). | Consultation 21 July 2026, the Academic Coordinator decides which professor teaches; Platform (current build), offering setup | Proposed |
| FAC-04 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | Availability is recorded as weekly hours with dated exceptions on top. An exception "available" replaces the weekly hours for that day; an exception "unavailable" removes the day or the stated hours. A weekend needs a dated entry. | Platform (current build), faculty availability and exceptions | Proposed |
| FAC-05 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | Hours that the system filled in by itself are never counted as availability the professor entered. Until the professor or GS staff enter hours, the record reads "not entered" and the professor is not suggested for a subject. | Platform (current build), faculty availability status | Proposed |
| FAC-06 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | The Research Coordinator and GS staff may request availability from the panel members for a student's defense. A request has a date window, an optional message and a status of Open, Answered or Cancelled, and it is answered when the professor enters hours or confirms the hours on record. | Platform (current build), availability requests; Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, scheduling | Proposed |
| FAC-07 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | A professor may be assigned at most 24 teaching units in a semester. An assignment that would pass the limit is refused and the Academic Coordinator chooses another professor or changes the sections. The 24-unit value is a prototype value pending the Graduate School's answer (OQ-45). | Platform (current build), faculty teaching load limit | Proposed |
| FAC-08 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | Only an Active professor of the same college as the subject's program may be assigned to the subject. | Platform (current build), offering setup | Proposed |
| FAC-09 | Faculty course preference and teaching load | The Dean approves the offering plan, including its assigned professors, before it is published (CRS-10). GS-F-48 is the paper record of the assignment; the professor acknowledges it before classes begin. | Group working notes, BPM accurate flow, section 3; Platform (current build), offering plan decision | Proposed |
| ACC-01 | Platform accounts and roles | The platform has seven roles: Graduate School Staff, Academic Coordinator, Research Coordinator, Dean, Student, Faculty Member and Administrator. One account has one role and belongs to one person. | Platform (current build), role labels | Proposed |
| ACC-02 | Platform accounts and roles | Each screen and each action is open only to the roles that GS-P-05 lists for it (least privilege). A Student sees only the student's own record; a Faculty Member sees only the faculty member's own portal, panel invitations and adviser requests. | Platform (current build), role checks on every request | Proposed |
| ACC-03 | Platform accounts and roles | A student account is created when the student is handed off from admission (the monitoring workbook import). Each new student account gets its own initial password, shown once to the staff member who imported the file. | Platform (current build), student handoff import | Proposed |
| ACC-04 | Platform accounts and roles | A faculty account is created together with the faculty profile by GS staff or the Academic Coordinator. The email must not belong to another profile or account. | Platform (current build), faculty creation | Proposed |
| ACC-05 | Platform accounts and roles | An account for GS staff, a coordinator or the Dean is requested on GS-F-46, endorsed by the head of the person's office, approved by the Dean and set up by GS staff or the platform administrator within three working days of the approval (proposed). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| ACC-06 | Platform accounts and roles | A password set for someone else is temporary: the holder must choose a personal password at first sign-in before anything else works. A personal password has at least ten characters, differs from the old one and is not the email address or a shared demo password. | Platform (current build), change of password | Proposed |
| ACC-07 | Platform accounts and roles | Repeated failed sign-ins slow the next attempt (after five failures within 15 minutes the wait is 2 seconds and doubles to at most 30 seconds); the account is not locked. | Platform (current build), sign-in brake | Proposed |
| ACC-08 | Platform accounts and roles | An account is deactivated, not deleted, when the person leaves or changes office, within five working days of the request. A deactivated account cannot sign in. Setting a faculty profile to Inactive deactivates its login. | Platform (current build), account status; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| ACC-09 | Platform accounts and roles | The Dean reviews the list of active accounts and roles once a year (the interval is proposed, OQ-46). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| ACC-10 | Platform accounts and roles | The Administrator account is held by the person who maintains the platform and not by an office holder. Accounts are personal: a password is never shared, and a shared demonstration login exists only in demonstration mode. | Platform (current build), administrator and demonstration accounts; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| EAP-01 | Electronic approval and signatures | The e-signature already used on research forms by email (ADV-09, PRP-02, FIN-12) remains valid. GS-P-03 adds the approval recorded in the platform and does not replace the Protocol. | Platform (current build), approval records; Research Protocol AY2024-2025, e-signatures by email, as in ADV-09, PRP-02 and FIN-12 | Proposed |
| EAP-02 | Electronic approval and signatures | An electronic document and an electronic signature have legal recognition under RA 8792 (section 7, electronic documents; section 8, electronic signatures). An electronic signature equals a written signature when a method identifies the person to be bound and shows that the person had access to the document to consent or approve it, the method is dependable for its purpose, the person applied the signature to proceed, and the other party can verify it. The University must confirm that the platform's sign-in meets this test (OQ-47). | Republic Act No. 8792 (Electronic Commerce Act of 2000), s. 7 and s. 8 | Proposed |
| EAP-03 | Electronic approval and signatures | An approval recorded in the platform by the signed-in holder of the approving role is that officer's signature on the matching GS-F form, if the approval record shows the officer's name and role, the date and time, the decision and the remarks. | Republic Act No. 8792 (Electronic Commerce Act of 2000), s. 8; Platform (current build), approval records | Proposed |
| EAP-04 | Electronic approval and signatures | An officer approves only under the officer's own account and never lends a password. A representative of an officer (for example the Dean's authorised representative) signs in under the representative's own account, and the decision names the officer for whom the representative acts and the authority. | Republic Act No. 8792 (Electronic Commerce Act of 2000), s. 8; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| EAP-05 | Electronic approval and signatures | Every approve, endorse, return or decline control in the platform shows a one-line note that the decision is an electronic approval under GS-P-03. | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| EAP-06 | Electronic approval and signatures | The approval record is kept for as long as the case file it belongs to (PRV-08). It is not edited. A wrong approval is corrected by a new, later entry that states the reason, and both entries stay. | Platform (current build), transaction log | Proposed |
| EAP-07 | Electronic approval and signatures | Where the Protocol requires an e-signature on the form itself, the form keeps it. The platform approval does not take its place until the Graduate School confirms GS-P-03. | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| EAP-08 | Electronic approval and signatures | Other offices, including the Registrar, receive a platform approval only as a printed or exported case record, or as the form carrying the line "Approved electronically under GS-P-03 by [name, role] on [date, time]". | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| PRV-01 | Data privacy and records retention | The Graduate School keeps personal data of students, faculty and officers for the lifecycle processes in this manual. The University's Data Protection Officer oversees the Graduate School's handling of that data, and the Dean is accountable for the Graduate School's records. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 21; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| PRV-02 | Data privacy and records retention | The Graduate School collects only the data a process in this manual needs and uses it only for that process (transparency, legitimate purpose and proportionality).
