Official identity, enrollment and grade data come from AIMS and are not retyped (3.3). | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 11 | Proposed |
| PRV-03 | Data privacy and records retention | At first sign-in the platform shows the Data Privacy Notice (GS-P-04) and records the account holder's acceptance with the date, time and notice version. A person without an account, or who prefers paper, signs GS-F-45. A new version of the notice is shown again at the next sign-in. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 12; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| PRV-04 | Data privacy and records retention | A data subject may ask to be told what is held, to see and correct it, to object to its use, and to have it blocked or removed where the law and the retention schedule allow (rights of the data subject). The Graduate School answers within 15 working days (the period is proposed, OQ-48). | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 16 | Proposed |
| PRV-05 | Data privacy and records retention | Personal data are shown only to the roles that need them for their step (4.21). Staff do not keep copies on personal devices or send them through personal email or chat. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 20; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| PRV-06 | Data privacy and records retention | The platform protects personal data with role checks on every request, hashed passwords, a sign-in brake, session cookies that scripts cannot read, an audit log of changes, and uploaded files that are only given to roles allowed to see the case. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 20; Platform (current build), security measures | Proposed |
| PRV-07 | Data privacy and records retention | Any suspected breach is reported the same day to the Data Protection Officer and the Dean. The officer decides whether the National Privacy Commission and the affected persons must be notified. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 20 | Proposed |
| PRV-08 | Data privacy and records retention | Retention (proposed): a student's case file, the forms, the approval records and the transaction log are kept for 5 years after graduation or after the last enrollment, whichever applies. The graduation list and the transmittals to the Registrar are kept permanently. After the period the files are disposed of securely or kept only without names for reports (OQ-49). | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 11; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| PRV-09 | Data privacy and records retention | Data go to an outside service only if GS-P-04 names the service and what it receives. The platform can use an outside search service for the policy question box and for reading documents; when it is used, text from the question, the documents or the person's own record leaves the platform. Switching it off must leave the platform working with its own documents (OQ-48). A faculty member's own calendar is linked only when the member connects it. | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 11 and s. 21; Platform (current build), outside services | Proposed |
| DEX-01 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | AIMS stays an external system and the official record of identity, enrollment and grades. The platform has no live link, no AIMS login and no screen reading of AIMS; only files are exchanged. | Consultation 21 July 2026, the group was told the platform cannot be integrated with AIMS; Platform (current build), AIMS boundary | Proposed |
| DEX-02 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | Files come in by upload by GS staff or the Academic Coordinator: the student monitoring workbook (.xlsx or .xlsm) at handoff, and class lists (.csv or .xlsx with a faculty column) for enrollment tagging. The platform validates a file, refuses rows it cannot accept (for example a row for another semester, or for a student who is on leave, AWOL or withdrawn) and never changes grades. | Platform (current build), student handoff and class list import | Proposed |
| DEX-03 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | The proposed AIMS export contract (version PROPOSED-1.0, Documents/Sample_Data/AIMS_Export/) is a workbook with the sheets Export_Metadata, Students, Enrollment_Subjects, Grades and Subject_Offerings. STUDENT_NUMBER is the identity key; official values are kept as exported. The exact columns, the exporting office and the cadence are for the AIMS and Registrar owner to confirm (OQ-50). The current build does not yet read this workbook. | Platform (current build), Documents/Sample_Data/AIMS_Export | Proposed |
| DEX-04 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | The files from AIMS or the Registrar are supplied at the start of each term, after enrollment closes, and again after grades are posted (proposed). Between those dates the platform sends changes out by the daily change report (DEX-06). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| DEX-05 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | Identity, enrollment, grade and completion values imported from AIMS are read-only. If the Graduate School's record differs, the difference is flagged for follow-up and corrected in AIMS, and a replacement export is imported with its history kept. | This manual, section 3.3; Platform (current build), read-only official data | Proposed |
| DEX-06 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | Every change made in the platform that the Registrar must apply in AIMS (enrollment or subject change, withdrawal, course adjustment, leave, readmission, AWOL or residency, graduation) appears on a dated daily change report. The Academic Coordinator or GS staff apply it in AIMS and mark it reflected; the platform does not write to AIMS. | Platform (current build), daily change report | Proposed |
| DEX-07 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | Only leave and readmission cases that the Dean has approved can be exported to the Registrar list. Each export is logged with the file name, the person and the time, and a case moves from Pending Handoff to Exported - Ready to Send, then to Sent to Registrar and Acknowledged when GS staff record them (see GRD-16, OQ-32). | Platform (current build), leave and readmission Registrar export | Proposed |
| DEX-08 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | The graduation list is exported only for endorsements the Dean has approved and for students who are still eligible when the file is made. It is a CSV with batch, student number, name, program, coursework status, research status, endorsement status, the Dean's approval date and remarks, and the Registrar export status, and the Dean emails it manually (GRD-16). | Platform (current build), endorsed graduation list | Proposed |
| DEX-09 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | An outbound file holds only the columns the Registrar needs and goes by official University email, never by personal email or chat (PRV-05). | Republic Act No. 10173 (Data Privacy Act of 2012), s. 11; Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |
| DEX-10 | AIMS and Registrar data exchange | Each outbound file is sent with a Records Transmittal (GS-F-44). The transmittal and the Registrar's acknowledgement, when one is given, are filed (OQ-32). | Group proposal, forms pack revision of 2026-11-04 | Proposed |

## 6 Open questions for validation

The group could not answer the following questions from the sources. Each states where the sources conflict, where they are silent, or where practice differs from the Handbook, gives the options the group can see, and leaves a blank for the Graduate School's answer. Where a question affects rules, the rule numbers are listed. Until a question is answered, the platform follows the Handbook. Where that ruling has already settled what the platform does (for example the withdrawal window and fees, the length of a leave, or whether a leave counts toward maximum residence), the list of sources says how the platform now works; the question stays open so that the Graduate School can confirm the rule or choose another.

### OQ-01 Withdrawal of subject: how long is it allowed and what does it cost?

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 49, 51: a student may withdraw subjects until the second week from the start of classes; 10% of the total amount due for the term is charged in the first week and 20% in the second week.
- Consultation 29 July 2026 (the group's account of the Associate Dean's answer): withdrawal is possible before classes start or in the first week; Consultation 21 July 2026 describes withdrawal as the official, no-mark kind of leaving a subject.
- Platform now (implements option A): a withdrawal is accepted before classes begin and until the end of the second week (14 calendar days from the semester start date); the fee tier for the date of the request is shown as information only (10% in the first week, 20% in the second, full fees after the window); the Business Office collects. The earlier build's seven-day, no-penalty rule came from the consultation account and was replaced.
- Owner's ruling for this revision: the Graduate School Handbook 2022-2023 is in force, so the platform follows it wherever it is explicit. The question stays so that the Graduate School can confirm the rule or choose another.

**Question.** Which window and which fee apply to a withdrawal of a subject?

**Options**

- A. The Handbook as written: until the end of the second week, 10% in week 1 and 20% in week 2.
- B. Before classes begin and the first week only, with the fee to be stated by the Graduate School.
- C. Two steps: no academic mark in the first seven days, and the Handbook's 10% and 20% fees through the second week.

**Affects rules:** WD-01, WD-02, WD-13, WD-14

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-02 What does a withdrawal before the first day of classes cost?

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 49, 51: states a 10% charge "within the first week of classes" but nothing about a request made before classes begin.
- Platform now: a request before classes begin is accepted and no amount is shown; the screen says that the Handbook states none and that the Business Office confirms it.

**Question.** Is there a charge when a student withdraws a subject after enrolling but before classes start?

**Options**

- A. No charge.
- B. 10% as in the first week.
- C. The Business Office decides case by case.

**Affects rules:** WD-02, WD-14

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-03 After the second week: may a student still withdraw from one subject, and what is it called and marked?

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49: "The student retains the right to withdraw all subjects at any time of the semester thereafter but will pay the full enrolment fees." It speaks of all subjects, not one.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53: grade symbols W (Withdrawn) and D (Dropped); a leave taken in the second half of the semester gives W for all courses.
- Consultation 21 July 2026: dropping is leaving a subject in the middle of the semester; withdrawal is the official, earlier kind.
- Platform now (implements option A): after the second week a single subject cannot be withdrawn; the message tells the student that all subjects may still be withdrawn at full fees, or a leave of absence filed.

**Question.** After the second week, what may a student do with a single subject, and what mark and fee apply?

**Options**

- A. Only a leave of absence or withdrawal of all subjects; single subjects cannot be withdrawn.
- B. A single subject may be dropped (mark D) with full fees.
- C. A single subject may be withdrawn (mark W) with full fees, or fees to the last month of attendance for a justifiable reason.

**Affects rules:** WD-03, WD-04, DRP-10

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-04 Does a withdrawal or a drop need approval, and may it be refused?

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 49: for a withdrawal the student writes a letter and seeks approval from the Dean.
- Consultation 29 July 2026 (account of the Associate Dean): the Dean decides with discretion; usual reasons to deny are a late request or an unsupported reason.
- Consultation 15 and 29 July 2026 (capstone adviser sessions, not Graduate School staff): dropping and withdrawing are a student's right and are not denied.
- Consultation 21 July 2026: for dropping, the student talks to the Academic Coordinator and, once agreed, the subject is tagged dropped.
- Platform now: the Dean approves or denies every withdrawal (a denial needs a reason); a drop is recorded by the Academic Coordinator without an approval step, and only when the unexcused absences entered are above 20%.

**Question.** Who approves a withdrawal, who approves a drop, and on what grounds may a request be refused?

**Options**

- A. The Dean approves every withdrawal and may refuse; a drop needs no approval.
- B. Neither needs approval; the Graduate School only records them.
- C. Withdrawal needs the Dean's approval; a drop needs the Academic Coordinator's agreement.

**Affects rules:** WD-06, WD-13, DRP-07, DRP-09

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-05 Dropping: procedure, forms, and how the Graduate School finds out

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023: the only dropping rule is the automatic drop for absences (p. 52); no student-requested drop procedure or form is described; the Withdrawal Form is named (p. 49) but not supplied.
- Consultation 21 July 2026: the student talks or writes to the Academic Coordinator; the Graduate School drops in AIMS as well as in its own record.
- Platform now: there is no student drop request; the Academic Coordinator records the drop with the absence percentage, a note and a date; nothing is sent to AIMS.

**Question.** What is the drop procedure, which form (if any) is used, who informs the professor and who records the drop in AIMS? How is the Graduate School informed if a student stops attending without telling anyone?

**Options**

- A. Email or conversation with the Academic Coordinator only; no form.
- B. A written form, to be supplied by the Graduate School.
- C. The professor reports drops to the Academic Coordinator.

**Affects rules:** DRP-07, DRP-08, DRP-09

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-06 DRP grade and program consequences

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52: an automatic drop for absences gives the grade DRP with a 5.0.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53: the grading table lists D (Dropped), not DRP.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 54: a 5.0 means the student is automatically dropped from the program; failure in any subject means no re-admission.
- Platform now: it does not produce the DRP grade or apply the retention rule; both are kept in the register as documented only.

**Question.** Is DRP the same as D? Does a DRP with 5.0 count as a failure that drops the student from the program?

**Options**

- A. DRP is a failure; the retention rule applies.
- B. DRP is recorded as a drop only; the retention rule does not apply.
- C. To be decided by the Dean case by case.

**Affects rules:** DRP-03, DRP-05, DRP-06

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-07 Change or add of subject after the first week

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 48-49: change of subject and adding of subjects are allowed during the first week only; no fee or exception is stated.
- Platform now: after the first week (7 calendar days from the semester start date) an addition or change is flagged and staff can save it only with a documented exception (the Associate Dean's written approval for an addition; subject dissolved, schedule conflict or failed prerequisite for a change); a student cannot do it in the portal.

**Question.** Is any exception possible after the first week, who may grant it, and is there a fee for changing or adding?

**Options**

- A. No exception.
- B. The Dean may allow an exception for a stated reason.
- C. Fees to be stated by the Business Office.

**Affects rules:** CRS-01, CRS-02, CRS-03

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-08 How are "first week" and "second week" counted?

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 49, 52: "first week of classes", "second week" and "attendance counted from the first regular class meeting"; no calendar rule is given.
- Platform now: calendar days from the start date of the semester: 7 for the first week (the fee tier and the add or change window) and 14 for the withdrawal window.

**Question.** Are the weeks counted from the first day of classes of the semester, or from each subject's first class meeting? Do summer terms follow the same weeks?

**Options**

- A. Calendar days from the official first day of classes.
- B. From the first class meeting of each subject.
- C. Another rule, to be stated.

**Affects rules:** WD-01, WD-02, CRS-01, CRS-02, ENR-03

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-09 Leave of absence: length and number of leaves

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52: a leave may be approved for one year and renewed for at most another year.
- Platform now (implements option A in part): up to two semesters per request and four semesters in total with renewals; the number of leaves is not limited; a semester is counted by its position in the list of semesters set up in the platform.
- Owner's ruling for this revision: the Graduate School Handbook 2022-2023 is in force, so the platform follows it wherever it is explicit. The question stays so that the Graduate School can confirm the rule or choose another.

**Question.** How long may a leave be, is the two-year limit counted per leave or over the whole program, is a year two semesters or three terms, and is there a maximum number of leaves?

**Options**

- A. One year, renewable once for another year, per leave; no limit on the number of leaves.
- B. A total of two years over the whole program.
- C. Two semesters, renewable once, with a maximum number of leaves to be stated.

**Affects rules:** LOA-03, LOA-13

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-10 Does leave of absence count toward maximum residence?

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 55: a master's program must be finished within seven academic years including leave of absence; a doctorate within nine including leave.
- Platform now (implements option A): time on leave counts; the residency clock is never paused (register value residency.includes_loa).
- Owner's ruling for this revision: the Graduate School Handbook 2022-2023 is in force, so the platform follows it wherever it is explicit. The question stays so that the Graduate School can confirm the rule or choose another.

**Question.** Is the leave included in the maximum residence as the Handbook says?

**Options**

- A. Yes, included (Handbook).
- B. No, the clock is paused.
- C. The first year of a leave is excluded.

**Affects rules:** LOA-10, RES-07, RES-09

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

### OQ-11 Leave of absence: filing deadline and start term

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 52: "LOA must be done on or before the deadline" (no date is given).
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53: a leave is allowed in the second half of a semester (class standing W, no refund) but not within two weeks before the last day of classes.
- Platform now (implements option B in part): a leave may start in a running semester until 14 days before its last day of classes; in the second half the courses become W with no refund (shown as a note only); a semester already over is refused. The student form still lists only future semesters.

**Question.** What is the deadline to file a leave, and may a leave start in the semester already running?

**Options**

- A. Deadline is the end of the enrollment period; the leave starts in a future term only.
- B. As in the Handbook: a leave may start during the semester until two weeks before the last day of classes, with W and no refund in the second half.
- C. Another rule.

**Affects rules:** LOA-04, LOA-08, LOA-09, LOA-13

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Decided by / date:** ____________________

**Question.** May a structured form replace the letter, and may a student withdraw a pending request?

- A. Letter only.
- B. The platform form is accepted as the written request.
- C. Form plus a signed letter attached.

*Kind:* Conflict.

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 45: clearance from the Business Office, an interview (reason for delay, residency requirements), evaluation of subject and grade requirements, and an Advise Slip from the Dean.
- Platform: structured return intention, updated study plan, program or adviser consultation, and no pending accountability.
- Consultation 20 July 2026: the adviser asked whether a readmitted student is automatically eligible to enroll; no answer is recorded.

**Question.** Which list applies, who interviews and evaluates, and is a readmitted student automatically cleared to enroll?

**Options**

- A. The Handbook's four requirements only.
- B. The platform's four items only.

### OQ-14 AWOL: who declares it and what follows

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 53: a student who withdraws without a formal leave is AWOL, and registration privileges are curtailed or withdrawn; nothing is said about who declares it, when, or whether AWOL counts toward maximum residence.
- Platform now: AWOL is flagged from source evidence (an imported AWOL standing, or a semester marked Withdrawn with no leave and no resolved return) and cannot be declared manually; the AWOL period counts toward maximum residence because the clock is never paused.

**Question.** Who declares AWOL, after how long, is the Registrar told, and does the AWOL period count toward maximum residence?

**Options**

- A. The Dean declares it after one semester with no enrollment and no approved leave; the period counts toward maximum residence.
- B. The Registrar's record is the only basis; the Graduate School only records it.
- C. Another rule.

**Affects rules:** AWL-01, AWL-04, AWL-05, AWL-06

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

**Question.** What is the fee, what is the registration deadline, and how many residency semesters may a student take?

- A. As for a regular semester; no limit other than maximum residence.

### OQ-16 Maximum residence: how it is measured and who approves the extension

*Kind:* Silent.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, pp. 54-55: candidacy within three (master's) or five (doctorate) academic years from admission, completion within five or seven, an extension of up to two years with a graded six-unit refresher course, and absolute limits of seven and nine academic years; no rule says whether summer terms count or who approves an extension.
- Platform now: counts academic years from the entry academic year (year 1), stepping up each June, never reduced by leave; this can differ by up to a year from a count from the date of admission.

**Question.** How is an academic year counted (summer terms?), and who approves an extension and the refresher course?

**Options**

- A. Academic years from the date of admission; the Dean approves the extension.
- B. Academic years from the date of first enrollment; the Academic Coordinator recommends and the Dean approves.
- C. Another rule.

**Affects rules:** RES-05, RES-06, RES-07, RES-08, RES-09

**Stakeholder's answer:** ____________________________________________

### OQ-17 Comprehensive examination: before or after the title defense and the adviser?

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 57: after the student passes the comprehensive examination the student confers with the Dean for an adviser and is advanced to candidacy.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58: an adviser may be assigned while the student is still taking courses.
- Research Protocol AY2024-2025: silent on the comprehensive examination.
- Platform now: all research stages from the title defense onward stay locked until the examination is Passed and all curriculum subjects are complete; the Graduate School can switch the examination requirement off in the register.

**Question.** In what order must the comprehensive examination, the adviser designation and the title defense happen, and does it differ for thesis and non-thesis tracks?

**Options**

- A. The examination must be passed before the title defense and the adviser.
- B. An adviser and the title defense may come during coursework; the examination only before the final defense.
- C. Different order for thesis and non-thesis students.

**Affects rules:** CEX-10, CEX-13, ADV-08

### OQ-19 Who approves the designation of the research adviser?

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58: the Dean appoints the adviser, with the approval of the program adviser and the Dean, giving primary importance to the student's choice.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59: the Dean, the Associate Dean and the Research Coordinator make the final decision.
- Research Protocol AY2024-2025, Designation of the Research Adviser: the Research Coordinator emails Form 3 to the Dean for deliberation; the Research Coordinator, Associate Dean and Dean sign.

**Question.** Who decides, and who signs: the Dean alone, or the Dean, Associate Dean and Research Coordinator together? Is the program adviser's approval needed?

**Options**

- A. The Dean, Associate Dean and Research Coordinator decide together; the program adviser is consulted.
- B. The Dean decides on the Research Coordinator's recommendation.
- C. Another route.

### OQ-20 Adviser eligibility and the limit on advisees

*Kind:* Conflict.

**What the sources say**

- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 58: a Graduate School faculty with a doctoral degree aligned with the discipline and at least one refereed publication or recognized output; more than five advisees only with the Dean's approval.
- Handbook 2022-2023, p. 59: a maximum of five advisees per semester.
- Research Protocol AY2024-2025: a PhD for PhD students and at least PhD units for MA students; a maximum of five active advisees for the current academic year.
- Platform now: the five-advisee limit is in the register as a documented rule only; the platform does not count advisees or check qualifications.

*Kind:* Silent.

- Platform now: the officer's own account and password identify the officer; the log records the decision; there is no digital certificate and no second sign-in step.

## Appendix A Forms crosswalk

Every form and process document of the forms pack, in the order GS-P-01 and GS-P-02 (forms control), GS-F-01 to GS-F-44 (lifecycle), GS-P-03 to GS-P-06 and GS-F-45 to GS-F-48 (platform basis) and GS-F-49 to GS-F-54 (supplementary forms). Chapter is the section of Chapter 4 in which the form is used (1.4 is the forms control section of Chapter 1). "Official by name" means the Handbook or the Research Protocol names the form; the Graduate School's own template was not supplied to the group, so the group drafted it. "Proposed" means the group added the form where the sources name none. The printed forms are in Documents/Graduate_School/Forms/, one folder per chapter.

|---|---|---|---|---|

## Appendix B Glossary

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| AC | Academic Coordinator. |
| AIMS | The University's academic information management system used by the Registrar for admission, enrollment and grades. |
| Advice Slip | A slip issued by the Graduate School Office (or by the Dean for returnees) that allows a subject to be added or a returning student to enroll. |
| Approved absence | Absence for a school-sponsored activity or official representation of the University, approved by the Dean, not counted against the allowed absences. |
| Bridging course | Subjects a student must take to make up for an insufficient background, for example six units of thesis for an applicant with a non-thesis master's degree. |
| Closed-door final defense | The first final oral defense of a thesis or dissertation before the panel, without an audience. |
| CHED CMO 15 | CHED Memorandum Order No. 15, series of 2019, cited by the Handbook and the Protocol for adviser qualifications. |
| Cognate | An elective group of subjects in a graduate curriculum. |
| DRP | The grade symbol given when a student is automatically dropped from a course for absences (Handbook p. 52); the grading table also lists the symbol D (Dropped). |
| 14-Day Rule | At least fourteen days, inclusive of weekends, from the date the panel members receive the manuscript to the date of the oral defense. |
| GS | Graduate School of the University of St. La Salle. |
| GS Research Committee | The committee of the Dean, Associate Dean, Academic Coordinator and Research Coordinator that deliberates on panel and research matters (Research Protocol, Proposal Defense, Part I, step 4; Handbook p. 75 lists the Research Coordinator, Academic Coordinator, Associate Dean and an external expert). |
| INC | Incomplete grade. Must be removed within one academic year. |
| IRF | USLS Institutional Research Format applied by the editor. |
| LOA | Leave of absence. |
| Maximum residence | The longest period a student may take to finish a program: master's seven academic years, doctorate nine, in each case including leave of absence. |
| MOA | Memorandum of agreement with a practicum site. |
| Panel chair | The university-affiliated panel member who hosts the defense and emails the consolidated forms. |
| Project paper | The final requirement of a non-thesis master's option (for example the MBA project paper). |
| PEO | Publication and Engagement Office. |
| RC | Research Coordinator. |
| Residency enrollment | Enrollment with no subjects, allowed only for thesis or dissertation writing, practicum or internship, completing an incomplete grade, the comprehensive examination, or waiting for a research publication. |
| RERC / RERO / SRERO | Research ethics committee and office. The Handbook uses Social Research Ethics Committee and Office; the Protocol uses RERC and RERO. Whether these are the same office is an open question. |
| SIR | Similarity index rating from Turnitin. Not more than 15% is required. |
| Subject | A course in the curriculum. A program is a degree. |
| TOR | Transcript of record. |
| W | Grade symbol for a withdrawn subject. |
| AWOL | Absent without leave: withdrawing from the University without a formal leave of absence. |
